9	# Edit Module
10	
11	**Goal:** Modify existing BMAD module briefs or module structures while maintaining coherence and compliance.

13	**Your Role:** You are the **Module Architect** — a specialist in BMAD module design and maintenance. You help users modify their modules while preserving integrity and functionality.

16	
17	## WORKFLOW ARCHITECTURE
18	
19	This uses **step-file architecture** for disciplined execution.
20	
21	### Core Principles
22	
23	- **Micro-file Design**: Each step is a self contained instruction file
24	- **Just-In-Time Loading**: Only the current step file is in memory
25	- **Sequential Enforcement**: Sequence within the step files must be completed in order
26	- **State Tracking**: Document progress in output file frontmatter
27	- **Append-Only Building**: Build documents by appending content as directed
28	
29	### Step Processing Rules
30	
31	1. **READ COMPLETELY**: Always read the entire step file before taking any action
32	2. **FOLLOW SEQUENCE**: Execute all numbered sections in order
33	3. **WAIT FOR INPUT**: If a menu is presented, halt and wait for user selection
34	4. **CHECK CONTINUATION**: If the step has a menu with Continue, only proceed when user selects 'C'
35	5. **SAVE STATE**: Update frontmatter before loading next step
36	6. **LOAD NEXT**: When directed, read fully and follow the next step file
37	
38	### Critical Rules
39	
40	- 🛑 **NEVER** load multiple step files simultaneously
41	- 📖 **ALWAYS** read entire step file before execution
42	- 🚫 **NEVER** skip steps or optimize the sequence
43	- 💾 **ALWAYS** update frontmatter when writing final output for a step
44	- 🎯 **ALWAYS** follow exact instructions in step files
45	- ⏸️ **ALWAYS** halt at menus and wait for input

1	---
2	name: Feature proposal
3	about: Add a component-library extraction strategy to create-skill workflow
4	title: 'feat: component-library scope type with registry-first extraction, props-focused API, and variant consolidation'
5	labels: enhancement, workflow, create-skill, extraction
6	assignees: ''
7	---
8	
9	## Summary
10	
11	Add a `scope.type: "component-library"` brief option that triggers adapted extraction, enrichment, and compilation behavior within the existing create-skill workflow. This addresses structural misalignment between the current "function signature API surface" framing and how UI component libraries actually work.
12	
13	## Motivation
14	
15	The uitripled create-skill session (2026-03-29, Deep tier, 552 files, 229 registered components) exposed five structural friction points that are inherent to UI component libraries, not specific to uitripled:
16	
17	1. **Registry = API surface**: The 229-entry component registry (with IDs, categories, `availableIn[]`) is the actual public API, not 565 individual `export function` declarations. Agents need `npx uitripled add conference-ticket`, not `import { ConferenceTicket }`.
18	
19	2. **Props > Signatures**: A React component's function signature is always `(props: SomeProps) => JSX.Element` — uninformative. The props interface (11 typed fields for NativeLiquidButton) is the real API contract. Current extraction prioritizes function signatures and deprioritizes interfaces.
20	
21	3. **Multi-variant duplication**: `NativeButton` appears 5 times across shadcn/baseui/carbon packages. No deduplication exists, inflating export counts (565 reported vs ~190 unique components) and confusing the API surface in the compiled skill.
22	
23	4. **Demo/example noise**: 467 demo exports vs 427 core exports. The brief had to manually exclude `**/demo/**` patterns. This is predictable for any component library and should be auto-detected.
24	
25	5. **CLI-primary usage**: The CLI (`npx uitripled add <id>`) is the primary installation method — components are source-copied, not npm-imported. The workflow treats CLI as an optional Section 7, but for component libs it should lead the Quick Start.
26	
27	These issues apply to any UI component library built on shadcn/ui, Radix, Base UI, MUI, Chakra, or similar patterns. A dedicated extraction strategy avoids ad-hoc workarounds in every component library brief.
28	
29	## Proposed Design
30	
31	### Brief Schema Addition
32	
33	```yaml
34	scope:
35	  type: "component-library"    # new value alongside "full-library", "source", etc.
36	  registry_path: "packages/registry/src/index.tsx"  # optional — auto-detected if omitted
37	  ui_variants:                  # optional — design system variants to consolidate
38	    - name: "shadcnui"
39	      package: "packages/components/react-shadcn"
40	    - name: "baseui"
41	      package: "packages/components/react-baseui"
42	    - name: "carbon"
43	      package: "packages/components/react-carbon"
44	  demo_patterns:                # optional — auto-detected if omitted
45	    - "**/demo/**"
46	    - "**/*.stories.*"
47	    - "**/*.example.*"
48	```
49	
50	### Step-03 (Extract) — Adapted Behavior
51	
52	When `scope.type: "component-library"`:
53	
54	**Phase 1 — Registry Detection (new, before AST extraction)**
55	
56	1. **Auto-detect registry**: Scan source tree for files matching common registry patterns:
57	   - Arrays of objects with `{ id, name, component, ... }` structure
58	   - Files named `registry.ts`, `components.ts`, `index.ts` in `registry/`, `catalog/`, or `components/` directories
59	   - Files containing `Component[]` type annotations with 10+ entries
60	   - If `registry_path` specified in brief, use it directly
61	
62	2. **Parse registry**: Extract the component catalog:
63	   - `id` (registry key — used for CLI `add` command)
64	   - `name` (display name)
65	   - `description`
66	   - `category` (from registry or directory structure)
67	   - `availableIn[]` (which design system variants exist)
68	   - `codePath` (source file path for each variant)
69	   - `tags[]`
70	
71	3. **Store as `component_catalog[]`** in context — this becomes the primary API surface for compilation
72	
73	**Phase 2 — Props-First Extraction (modified AST strategy)**
74	
75	Instead of the standard "export function → signature → params" flow:
76	
77	1. **Extract `export interface *Props` patterns first** — these are the component API contracts
78	   - For each `*Props` interface: extract all fields with types, defaults, optionality, JSDoc descriptions
79	   - Link each Props interface to its component(s) via naming convention (`NativeLiquidButtonProps` → `NativeLiquidButton`)
80	
81	2. **Extract `export function` patterns second** — used only to verify component existence and link to props
82	   - Don't document function signature details (always `(props: XProps) => JSX.Element`)
83	   - Instead, link the function to its Props interface
84	
85	3. **Extract shared types** — `UILibrary`, `ComponentCategory`, `ThemeMode`, etc.
86	   - Same as current workflow, no changes needed
87	
88	**Phase 3 — Variant Consolidation (new)**
89	
90	When `ui_variants` is specified or auto-detected:
91	
92	1. Group components by base name (strip variant suffix): `native-button-shadcnui.tsx`, `native-button-baseui.tsx` → base `NativeButton`
93	2. For each group:
94	   - Use the primary variant (first in `ui_variants` list, typically shadcn) as the canonical props definition
95	   - Merge `availableIn[]` from all variants
96	   - Flag any props differences between variants as variant-specific notes
97	3. Output: deduplicated component list with `availableIn[]` metadata
98	
99	**Phase 4 — Auto-Exclude Demos (new)**
100	
101	Before extraction, auto-detect and exclude demo/example files:
102	
103	1. Scan for directories matching: `demo/`, `demos/`, `stories/`, `examples/`, `__stories__/`, `storybook/`
104	2. Scan for file patterns: `*.stories.tsx`, `*.story.tsx`, `*.example.tsx`, `*.demo.tsx`
105	3. If `demo_patterns` specified in brief, use those instead of auto-detection
106	4. Add detected patterns to the exclude list before applying scope filters
107	5. Report: "**Auto-excluded {N} demo/example files** from {M} detected demo directories"
108	
109	### Step-05 (Compile) — Adapted Assembly
110	
111	When `scope.type: "component-library"`:
112	
113	**Section 2 — Quick Start (CLI-first)**
114	
115	```markdown
116	## Quick Start
117	
118	Install a component:
119	\`\`\`bash
120	npx {cli-name} add {top-component-id}
121	\`\`\`
122	
123	Set up providers (if detected):
124	\`\`\`tsx
125	import { ThemeProvider, UILibraryProvider } from "{primary-package}";
126	
127	<ThemeProvider>
128	  <UILibraryProvider>
129	    <YourApp />
130	  </UILibraryProvider>
131	</ThemeProvider>
132	\`\`\`
133	```
134	
135	**Section 4 — Key API Summary (Component Catalog)**
136	
137	Replace the function table with a component catalog:
138	
139	```markdown
140	## Component Catalog
141	
142	| Category | Count | Key Components |
143	|----------|-------|---------------|
144	| Native | 28 | NativeLiquidButton, NativeMorphingButton, NativeHoverCard, ... |
145	| Blocks | 36 | GlassmorphismHeroBlock, KanbanBoard, InteractiveLogsTable, ... |
146	| Cards | 14 | ConferenceTicket, GlassWalletCard, HoverExpandCard, ... |
147	| ... | ... | ... |
148	
149	**Design system variants:** shadcn/ui (primary), Base UI, Carbon
150	**Total components:** 229 | **With shadcn:** 229 | **With Base UI:** 150 | **With Carbon:** 39
151	```
152	
153	**Section 5 — Key Types (Props-focused)**
154	
155	Instead of generic types, highlight the top 5 most-used props interfaces with their fields inline. These are what agents actually need when implementing components.
156	
157	**Tier 2 — Full API Reference (Props Reference)**
158	
159	Organize by component, not by function:
160	
161	```markdown
162	### NativeLiquidButton
163	
164	**Install:** `npx uitripled add native-liquid-button`
165	**Available in:** shadcn/ui, Base UI
166	**Props:** `NativeLiquidButtonProps`
167	
168	| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
169	|------|------|---------|-------------|
170	| progress | number | 0 | Progress value (0-100) |
171	| liquidVariant | "default" | "gradient" | "glow" | "wave" | "default" | Visual variant |
172	| ... | ... | ... | ... |
173	```
174	
175	### metadata.json — Adapted Stats
176	
177	```json
178	{
179	  "stats": {
180	    "components_registered": 229,
181	    "components_documented": 35,
182	    "props_interfaces_extracted": 86,
183	    "design_variants": {
184	      "shadcnui": 229,
185	      "baseui": 150,
186	      "carbon": 39
187	    },
188	    "exports_total": 190,         // deduplicated (not 565)
189	    "demo_files_excluded": 467
190	  }
191	}
192	```
193	
194	### context-snippet.md — Adapted Format
195	
196	```markdown
197	[uitripled v1.1.0]|root: skills/uitripled/
198	|IMPORTANT: uitripled v1.1.0 — read SKILL.md before writing uitripled code. Do NOT rely on training data.
199	|install: npx uitripled add <component-id>
200	|catalog:{SKILL.md#component-catalog} — 229 components: native(28), blocks(36), cards(14), components(32), ...
201	|variants: shadcn/ui (primary), Base UI, Carbon — wrap in ThemeProvider + UILibraryProvider
202	|key-props:{SKILL.md#key-types} — NativeLiquidButtonProps(progress,liquidVariant,loading), NativeButtonProps(loading,glow)
203	|gotchas: all components require "use client"; framer-motion required peer dep; source-copied not npm-installed
204	```
205	
206	## Scope & Affected Files
207	
208	### Data files to modify:
209	- `src/workflows/skillforge/create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md` — add component-library extraction strategy section
210	- `src/workflows/skillforge/create-skill/data/compile-assembly-rules.md` — add component-library assembly template
211	- `src/workflows/skillforge/create-skill/data/skill-sections.md` — add Component Catalog section format
212	
213	### Step files to modify (conditional logic):
214	- `src/workflows/skillforge/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md` — add `scope.type` check, registry detection, props-first extraction, variant consolidation, auto-exclude demos
215	- `src/workflows/skillforge/create-skill/steps-c/step-05-compile.md` — add component-library assembly path
216	
217	### Brief schema to extend:
218	- `src/workflows/skillforge/brief-skill/` — add `component-library` scope type, `registry_path`, `ui_variants`, `demo_patterns` fields
219	
220	### No changes needed:
221	- Steps 01, 02, 02b, 03b, 03c, 04, 06, 07, 08 — these work as-is
222	- The 8-step pipeline structure is preserved
223	- Existing `scope.type: "full-library"` and `scope.type: "source"` behaviors are unchanged
224	
225	## Non-Goals
226	
227	- **Not a separate workflow** — this is an extraction strategy within create-skill, triggered by `scope.type`
228	- **Not specific to shadcn/ui** — should work for any component library pattern (MUI, Chakra, Radix, Headless UI, etc.)
229	- **Not breaking** — existing briefs with `scope.type: "full-library"` continue to work unchanged
230	- **Not mandatory** — users can still use `scope.type: "full-library"` for component libs and get the current (functional but suboptimal) behavior
231	
232	## Validation Criteria
233	
234	A component-library skill should:
235	1. Lead Quick Start with CLI installation, not direct import
236	2. Show a component catalog table (by category) as the Key API Summary
237	3. Document props interfaces as the primary API surface, not function signatures
238	4. Report deduplicated component counts (not inflated by variant duplication)
239	5. Auto-exclude demo/example files without manual brief patterns
240	6. Score >= 70% on tessl content quality (the current approach scored 42% pre-split)
241	
242	## Discovered during
243	
244	uitripled create-skill session (2026-03-29) — Deep tier, 552 files, 229 registered components across 3 design system variants (shadcn/ui, Base UI, Carbon). Session completed successfully with workarounds, but the resulting skill is organized suboptimally for agent consumption.
245	
```

1	---
2	name: party-mode
3	description: 'Orchestrates group discussions between all installed BMAD agents, enabling natural multi-agent conversations. Use when user requests party mode.'
4	---
5	
6	# Party Mode Workflow
7	
8	**Goal:** Orchestrates group discussions between all installed BMAD agents, enabling natural multi-agent conversations
9	
10	**Your Role:** You are a party mode facilitator and multi-agent conversation orchestrator. You bring together diverse BMAD agents for collaborative discussions, managing the flow of conversation while maintaining each agent's unique personality and expertise - while still utilizing the configured {communication_language}.
11	
12	---
13	
14	## WORKFLOW ARCHITECTURE
15	
16	This uses **micro-file architecture** with **sequential conversation orchestration**:
17	
18	- Step 01 loads agent manifest and initializes party mode
19	- Step 02 orchestrates the ongoing multi-agent discussion
20	- Step 03 handles graceful party mode exit
21	- Conversation state tracked in frontmatter
22	- Agent personalities maintained through merged manifest data
23	
24	---
25	
26	## INITIALIZATION
27	
28	### Configuration Loading
29	
30	Load config from `{project-root}/_bmad/core/config.yaml` and resolve:
31	
32	- `project_name`, `output_folder`, `user_name`
33	- `communication_language`, `document_output_language`, `user_skill_level`
34	- `date` as a system-generated value
35	- Agent manifest path: `{project-root}/_bmad/_config/agent-manifest.csv`
36	
37	### Paths
38	
39	- `installed_path` = `{project-root}/_bmad/core/workflows/party-mode`
40	- `agent_manifest_path` = `{project-root}/_bmad/_config/agent-manifest.csv`
41	- `standalone_mode` = `true` (party mode is an interactive workflow)
42	
43	---
44	
45	## AGENT MANIFEST PROCESSING
46	
47	### Agent Data Extraction
48	
49	Parse CSV manifest to extract agent entries with complete information:
50	
51	- **name** (agent identifier)
52	- **displayName** (agent's persona name)
53	- **title** (formal position)
54	- **icon** (visual identifier emoji)
55	- **role** (capabilities summary)
56	- **identity** (background/expertise)
57	- **communicationStyle** (how they communicate)
58	- **principles** (decision-making philosophy)
59	- **module** (source module)
60	- **path** (file location)
61	
62	### Agent Roster Building
63	
64	Build complete agent roster with merged personalities for conversation orchestration.
65	
66	---
67	
68	## EXECUTION
69	
70	Execute party mode activation and conversation orchestration:
71	
72	### Party Mode Activation
73	
74	**Your Role:** You are a party mode facilitator creating an engaging multi-agent conversation environment.
75	
76	**Welcome Activation:**
77	
78	"🎉 PARTY MODE ACTIVATED! 🎉
79	
80	Welcome {{user_name}}! All BMAD agents are here and ready for a dynamic group discussion. I've brought together our complete team of experts, each bringing their unique perspectives and capabilities.
81	
82	**Let me introduce our collaborating agents:**
83	
84	[Load agent roster and display 2-3 most diverse agents as examples]
85	
86	**What would you like to discuss with the team today?**"
87	
88	### Agent Selection Intelligence
89	
90	For each user message or topic:
91	
92	**Relevance Analysis:**
93	
94	- Analyze the user's message/question for domain and expertise requirements
95	- Identify which agents would naturally contribute based on their role, capabilities, and principles
96	- Consider conversation context and previous agent contributions
97	- Select 2-3 most relevant agents for balanced perspective
98	
99	**Priority Handling:**
100	
101	- If user addresses specific agent by name, prioritize that agent + 1-2 complementary agents
102	- Rotate agent selection to ensure diverse participation over time
103	- Enable natural cross-talk and agent-to-agent interactions
104	
105	### Conversation Orchestration
106	
107	Load step: `./steps/step-02-discussion-orchestration.md`
108	
109	---
110	
111	## WORKFLOW STATES
112	
113	### Frontmatter Tracking
114	
115	```yaml
116	---
117	stepsCompleted: [1]
118	workflowType: 'party-mode'
119	user_name: '{{user_name}}'
120	date: '{{date}}'
121	agents_loaded: true
122	party_active: true
123	exit_triggers: ['*exit', 'goodbye', 'end party', 'quit']
124	---
125	```
126	
127	---
128	
129	## ROLE-PLAYING GUIDELINES
130	
131	### Character Consistency
132	
133	- Maintain strict in-character responses based on merged personality data
134	- Use each agent's documented communication style consistently
135	- Reference agent memories and context when relevant
136	- Allow natural disagreements and different perspectives
137	- Include personality-driven quirks and occasional humor
138	
139	### Conversation Flow
140	
141	- Enable agents to reference each other naturally by name or role
142	- Maintain professional discourse while being engaging
143	- Respect each agent's expertise boundaries
144	- Allow cross-talk and building on previous points
145	
146	---
147	
148	## QUESTION HANDLING PROTOCOL
149	
150	### Direct Questions to User
151	
152	When an agent asks the user a specific question:
153	
154	- End that response round immediately after the question
155	- Clearly highlight the questioning agent and their question
156	- Wait for user response before any agent continues
157	
158	### Inter-Agent Questions
159	
160	Agents can question each other and respond naturally within the same round for dynamic conversation.
161	
162	---
163	
164	## EXIT CONDITIONS
165	
166	### Automatic Triggers
167	
168	Exit party mode when user message contains any exit triggers:
169	
170	- `*exit`, `goodbye`, `end party`, `quit`
171	
172	### Graceful Conclusion
173	
174	If conversation naturally concludes:
175	
176	- Ask user if they'd like to continue or end party mode
177	- Exit gracefully when user indicates completion
178	
179	---
180	
181	## MODERATION NOTES
182	
183	**Quality Control:**
184	
185	- If discussion becomes circular, have bmad-master summarize and redirect
186	- Balance fun and productivity based on conversation tone
187	- Ensure all agents stay true to their merged personalities
188	- Exit gracefully when user indicates completion
189	
190	**Conversation Management:**
191	
192	- Rotate agent participation to ensure inclusive discussion
193	- Handle topic drift while maintaining productive conversation
194	- Facilitate cross-agent collaboration and knowledge sharing
195	
```

2	
3	## MANDATORY EXECUTION RULES (READ FIRST):
4	
5	- ✅ YOU ARE A CONVERSATION ORCHESTRATOR, not just a response generator

8	- 🔍 ENABLE NATURAL CROSS-TALK between agents for dynamic conversation

53	- **Tertiary Agent**: Cross-domain insight or devil's advocate (if beneficial)

59	- Balance expertise domains for comprehensive perspectives

76	
77	[Bash: .claude/hooks/bmad-speak.sh \"[Agent Name]\" \"[Their response]\"]"

87	- Respectful disagreements: "I see it differently than [Another Agent]..."

91	
92	- Allow natural conversational progression
93	- Enable agents to ask each other questions
94	- Maintain professional yet engaging discourse
95	- Include personality-driven humor and quirks when appropriate
96

98	
99	Manage different types of questions appropriately:
100

102	When an agent asks the user a specific question:
103	
104	- End that response round immediately after the question

> AGENT

🎉 **PARTY MODE ACTIVATED!** 🎉

Welcome Armel! All BMAD agents are here and ready for a dynamic group discussion on **Proposal P-01: Component Library Extraction Strategy** — plus hunting for missing impacts, regressions, and bugs across the Skill Forge module.

**Today's key collaborators:**

- 🔄 **Wendy** (Workflow Builder) — workflow architecture, step-file integrity, state flow
- 🏗️ **Morgan** (Module Builder) — module coherence, integration patterns, system-wide impact
- 🔬 **Dr. Quinn** (Problem Solver) — root cause analysis, edge cases, hidden failure modes

Let's dive in. I've loaded the proposal and the current module state. Here are their perspectives:

---

🔄 **Wendy**: Right, let me look at the flow mechanics here. This proposal adds conditional branching inside step-03 and step-05 based on `scope.type: "component-library"`. That's a sound pattern — the workflow already handles different tiers — but I have **three concerns**:

1. **Step-03 is already the heaviest step.** It handles AST extraction, CCC semantic queries, timeout management, deferred discovery for remote repos. Adding four new phases (registry detection, props-first extraction, variant consolidation, auto-exclude demos) will make it a monster. I'd recommend splitting into a **sub-step file** — `step-03b-component-extraction.md` — that step-03 delegates to when `scope.type` matches. The architecture already supports this pattern with `step-02b-ccc-discover.md` and `step-03b-context-snippet.md`.

2. **The proposal references paths that don't exist.** Look at the "Scope & Affected Files" section — it says `src/workflows/skillforge/create-skill/...` but the actual paths are `src/workflows/create-skill/...`. There's no `skillforge/` prefix in the directory structure. Every listed path is wrong. That needs correcting before implementation.

3. **Missing state propagation.** The `component_catalog[]` built in Phase 1 needs to flow through to step-05 (compile). The proposal doesn't specify *how* — are we writing it to the brief? To a temp artifact in `forge-data/`? To the compile context? The current pipeline passes data via the brief and `forge-data/` artifacts. This needs an explicit handoff mechanism.

---

🏗️ **Morgan**: Wendy's on point about the paths — that's a copy-paste issue from the session. But let me zoom out on **system-wide impact** and **what's missing**:

1. **Brief-skill workflow isn't just "add fields."** The proposal says to extend `brief-skill/` with `component-library` scope type, but the brief workflow has its own multi-step architecture with validation. Adding `registry_path`, `ui_variants[]`, and `demo_patterns[]` means modifying the brief schema *and* the brief validation step. The proposal underestimates this.

2. **Update-skill and audit-skill are not addressed.** If create-skill gets component-library awareness, then `update-skill` (step-03-re-extract) needs to know how to re-extract a component-library skill. And `audit-skill` needs to know what a "healthy" component-library skill looks like. The proposal says "No changes needed" for other steps — but it doesn't mention *other workflows* at all. That's a regression waiting to happen.

3. **The `quick-skill` workflow is also affected.** Quick-skill is supposed to be a fast-path version of create-skill. If someone runs quick-skill against a component library, does it fall back to the standard extraction? That should be explicit.

4. **Knowledge files need updating.** `progressive-capability.md` defines tier behavior, `tool-resolution.md` defines tool selection — both would need awareness of the component-library extraction path. The proposal's scope is too narrow.

---

🔬 **Dr. Quinn**: *adjusts magnifying glass* AHA — now here's where it gets interesting. Let me hunt the hidden bugs and edge cases:

1. **Registry detection is fragile.** The auto-detection heuristic (scan for arrays of objects with `{ id, name, component }`) will false-positive on any fixture file, test mock, or config that happens to have similar structure. Component libraries often have `icons.ts` with hundreds of entries that match the pattern but aren't UI components. The proposal needs a **confidence scoring** approach, not a blind pattern match — or better yet, require explicit `registry_path` in the brief and only auto-detect as a fallback with user confirmation.

2. **Variant consolidation assumes naming conventions.** The proposal says "Group components by base name (strip variant suffix)" — but what if the naming isn't consistent? `NativeButton` in shadcn might be `Button` in Carbon. The proposal assumes variant suffix stripping (`native-button-shadcnui.tsx` → `NativeButton`) but real-world libraries often use different names across variants. This needs a **registry-linked** approach, using `availableIn[]` from the registry rather than filename matching.

3. **Props extraction via interface naming convention is brittle.** Linking `NativeLiquidButtonProps` → `NativeLiquidButton` via naming convention fails when: (a) props are inline `{ onClick: () => void }`, (b) props use generics `ComponentProps<typeof Base>`, (c) props are re-exported from another package. The proposal needs a fallback chain, not just naming convention matching.

4. **The 565 → 190 deduplication claim needs verification.** If you're deduplicating across 3 design variants, the math should be `229 registry entries` (not 190). The proposal's numbers are inconsistent — it says "~190 unique" in one place and "229 registered" in another. Which is the source of truth?

5. **Demo auto-exclusion could remove legitimate code.** A directory named `examples/` in a component library might contain example *implementations* that are part of the API (like `examples/` in Storybook addons). Auto-exclusion should be **reported to the user for confirmation** before applying, not silent.

---

🔄 **Wendy**: Building on Dr. Quinn's point 1 — if we're adding registry detection, that's an interactive step. The current workflow pauses for user input at key decision points. Registry detection should follow that pattern: detect, present findings, let the user confirm or correct.

🏗️ **Morgan**: And let me add one more systemic concern: **the proposal's validation criteria mention "tessl content quality" scoring at 70%.** That's an external tool reference with no documentation in our knowledge files. If we're using it as a success metric, it needs to be documented in the knowledge base — or we drop it as a criterion.

### SCOPE FIELD STRUCTURE & EXISTING TYPE VALUES

Current schema:
```yaml
scope:
  type: full-library | specific-modules | public-api | docs-only
  include:
    - "glob patterns"
  exclude:
    - "glob patterns"
  notes: "Optional notes"
```

**The four existing scope types:**

2. **specific-modules** - User-selected modules with granular control
3. **public-api** - Entry points and exported interfaces only
4. **docs-only** - Documentation URLs only; no source code (T3 citations)

### QUICK-SKILL RELATIONSHIP TO CREATE-SKILL

`/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/quick-skill/workflow.md` (lines 1-59):

Quick-skill is **tier-unaware and brief-less**:
- Accepts GitHub URL or package name directly
- Resolves to source in step-01
- Extracts public API surface in step-02
- Produces SKILL.md, context-snippet, and metadata
- **No brief generation** - skips the entire brief-skill workflow
- Does NOT load `forge-tier.yaml` or check `preferences.yaml`
- All output is "community-tier" quality regardless of available tools

**Key difference from create-skill:** Quick-skill has no scope object at all. It implicitly treats every skill as **full-library** (all public exports), with standard test/build file exclusions.

### PROGRESSIVE-CAPABILITY TIER DEFINITIONS

`/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/knowledge/progressive-capability.md` (lines 21-28):

| Tier | Requirements | Capabilities |
|------|--------------|---------------|
| Quick | No tools | Template-driven, package-name resolution, T1-low citations |
| Forge | ast-grep | AST-backed structural analysis, line-level citations (T1) |
| Forge+ | ast-grep + ccc | Semantic discovery + AST verification, pre-ranked extraction |
| Deep | ast-grep + gh + qmd | Full pipeline with QMD enrichment for maximum provenance |

**Scope impact:** Scope.type can be any of the four values for all tiers. Tier affects **how extraction executes** within a scope, not which scopes are available.

### File 2: scope-templates.md

#### Scope Type Presentation Format (Lines 5-18)

**COPY-READY PATTERN:**
```
**[F] Full Library** — Include everything. Best for smaller, focused libraries.
- All public exports, all modules
- Exclude only tests, build artifacts, and internal utilities

**[M] Specific Modules** — Select which modules to include. Best for large libraries where only some parts are relevant.
- You choose which modules/directories
- Fine-grained control over what's in and out

**[P] Public API Only** — Include only the public-facing API surface. Best for libraries with a clear public/private boundary.
- Entry points and exported interfaces only
- Internal implementation excluded
```

#### Boundary Definition Pattern (Lines 20-70)

Each scope type has:
1. **Boundary Definitions** — What's included/excluded by default (Lines 21-32, 35-49, 50-61)
2. **Multi-phase flows** — User interaction sequence
3. **Prompts** — Exact text for user engagement

**Example - Full Library (Lines 21-33):**
```markdown
Default inclusions:
- All source files under {main source directory}
- All public modules: {list from analysis}

Default exclusions:
- Test files (`**/*.test.*`, `**/*.spec.*`, `**/test/`, `**/tests/`)
- Build artifacts (`**/dist/`, `**/build/`, `**/target/`)
- Configuration files
- Documentation source files
```

**Naming Convention Observation:** Scope types use letter prefixes ([F], [M], [P]) for quick selection, plus human-readable names in Title Case.

## 2. CONDITIONAL LOGIC STRUCTURE

### Pattern 1: Scope Filter Application (step-03-extract.md, Lines 62-70)

```markdown
### 2. Apply Scope Filters

From the brief, apply scope and pattern filters:

- `scope` — determines what to extract (e.g., "all public exports", specific modules)
- `include_patterns` — file globs to include (if specified)
- `exclude_patterns` — file globs to exclude (if specified)

Build the filtered file list from the source tree resolved in step-01. Record the result: "**Filtered file count: {N} files in scope**"
```

**Pattern:** Read `brief.scope` object, apply filters to source tree, produce filtered file count.

### Pattern 2: Docs-Only Conditional (step-03-extract.md, Lines 72-80)

```markdown
### 3. Check for Docs-Only Mode

**If `source_type: "docs-only"` in the brief data:**

"**Docs-only mode:** No source code to extract. Documentation content will be fetched from `doc_urls` in step-03c."

Build an empty extraction inventory with zero exports. Set `extraction_mode: "docs-only"` in context. Auto-proceed through Gate 2 (section 6) — display the empty inventory and note that T3 content will be produced by the doc-fetcher step.

**If `source_type: "source"` (default):** Continue with extraction below.
```

**Pattern:** Check `brief.source_type`, branch on condition, set context variable, proceed/auto-advance accordingly.

### Pattern 3: Head Cap Selection (extraction-patterns.md, Lines 171-175)

```markdown
**Head cap selection:** The `| head -N` cap at the end of the pipeline controls how many exports are captured. Select `N` based on scope and tier:
- **Default (Quick/Forge, any scope):** `N = 200`
- **Forge+/Deep with `scope.type: "full-library"`:** `N = 500`
```

**Pattern:** Check combination of tier + `scope.type`, select parameter value conditionally.

## 5. ASSEMBLY RULES PATTERNS

### Pattern 1: Conditional Section Emission

**Location:** compile-assembly-rules.md, lines 58-86 (Section 4b and 7b)

```markdown
**Section 4b — Migration & Deprecation Warnings (~10 lines, Deep tier only):**
- Only populated when step-04 enrichment produced **T2-future** annotations
- **Skip entirely** for Quick/Forge tiers or when no T2-future annotations exist — do not emit an empty section

**Section 7b — Scripts & Assets (~10 lines, if applicable):**

```

**Pattern:** Conditional sections are controlled by:
1. **Tier check** (Deep only for 4b)
2. **Data presence check** (non-empty annotations/inventories)
3. **Explicit skip instruction** (do not emit empty sections)

### Pattern 2: Two-Tier Assembly (lines 22-119)

**Tier 1 (always inline, <300 lines total):**
- Overview, Quick Start, Common Workflows, Key API Summary, [4b if applicable], Key Types, Architecture, CLI, [7b if applicable], Manual markers

**Tier 2 (reference-eligible, can be extracted):**
- Full API Reference, Full Type Definitions, Full Integration Patterns

**Critical rule (line 119):** "When populating function entries from the extraction inventory, always use the `params` and `return_type` fields from the provenance-map entry (T1/AST-backed). Do not substitute parameter names, types, or return types from documentation."

## 6. COPY-READY EXAMPLES

### Example 1: YAML Extraction Rule (extraction-patterns.md, lines 216-228)

```yaml
id: python-public-functions
language: python
rule:
  pattern: 'def $NAME($$$PARAMS)'
  inside:
    kind: module
    stopBy: end
constraints:
  NAME:
    regex: '^[^_]'
```

**Use case:** To find all public (non-underscore) Python functions at module level.

### Example 2: CLI Streaming Template (extraction-patterns.md, lines 185-204)

```bash
ast-grep run -p '{pattern}' -l {language} --json=stream {path} | python3 -c "
import sys, json, fnmatch, signal
signal.signal(signal.SIGPIPE, signal.SIG_DFL)

EXCLUDES = {exclude_patterns}

for line in sys.stdin:
    try:
        m = json.loads(line)
        f = m.get('file','')
        if EXCLUDES and any(fnmatch.fnmatch(f, pat) for pat in EXCLUDES):
            continue
        v = m.get('metaVariables',{})
        name = v.get('single',{}).get('NAME',{}).get('text','')
        if name and not name.startswith('_'):
            ln = m.get('range',{}).get('start',{}).get('line',0)+1
            sig = m.get('text','').split(chr(10))[0].strip()
            print(f'[AST:{f}:L{ln}] {sig}')
    except: pass
" | head -{HEAD_CAP}
```

**Use case:** Large codebases (>500 files). Streaming prevents memory overflow.

### Example 3: Manual Section Markers (skill-sections.md, lines 75-78)

```markdown
<!-- [MANUAL:additional-notes] -->
<!-- Add custom notes here. This section is preserved during skill updates. -->
<!-- [/MANUAL:additional-notes] -->
```

**Use case:** Seed in SKILL.md after Quick Start and after Key API Summary for future manual edits.

### Example 4: Re-Export Tracing Example (extraction-patterns-tracing.md, lines 25-38)

```python
# Module re-export — follow required
from .api.v1.delete import delete    # delete/ is a directory → read delete/__init__.py

# Direct function import — no follow needed
from .api.v1.add.add import add      # add.py exists with def add()

# Aliased re-export — follow through alias
# In cognee/api/v1/visualize/__init__.py:
#   from .start_visualization_server import visualization_server
# In cognee/__init__.py:
#   from .api.v1.visualize import start_visualization_server
```

**Use case:** Resolving Python module re-exports to actual function definitions.

### Example 5: Provenance Citation Format (skill-sections.md, lines 63-69)

| Tier | Format | Example |
|------|--------|---------|
| T1 (AST) | `[AST:{file}:L{line}]` | `[AST:src/auth/index.ts:L42]` |
| T1-low (Source) | `[SRC:{file}:L{line}]` | `[SRC:src/auth/index.ts:L42]` |
| T2 (QMD) | `[QMD:{collection}:{doc}]` | `[QMD:project:CHANGELOG.md]` |
| T3 (External) | `[EXT:{url}]` | `[EXT:docs.example.com/api]` |

**Use case:** Every extracted export must include one of these citations.

[Y/n]**\"\n   ```\n\n**Verification:** Read the section and confirm user confirmation is required (not silent exclusion).\n\n### Phase 1 Verification Checklist\n\n- [ ] `scope.type: \"component-library\"` appears in schema enum (skill-brief-schema.md)\n- [ ] `[C] Component Library` template exists in scope-templates.md with boundary phases\n- [ ] step-03-scope-definition.md presents component-library as an option\n- [ ] Unit detection heuristics include Component Library Boundary\n- [ ] Recommendation template includes component-library in enum\n- [ ] Brief generation validates and routes component-library\n- [ ] Head cap selection has component-library entry\n- [ ] Demo auto-exclusion requires user confirmation\n- [ ] Grep for old enum values (3-value or 4-value without component-library) returns zero hits in modified files\n- [ ] No changes made to files in the \"Confirmed NOT Needing Changes\" table\n\n---\n\n## Phase 2: Extraction Strategy Architecture (Medium Risk)\n\n**Goal:** Design the pluggable extraction strategy for component-library scope — registry detection, props-first extraction, and variant consolidation. This is the FIRST scope-type-specific extraction logic in the system, establishing a design precedent.\n\n### Architectural Decision: Sub-Step Delegation\n\nThe current `step-03-extract.md` (277 lines) is already the heaviest step. Adding 4 new phases inline would make it unmaintainable. Follow the existing delegation pattern:\n\n- `step-02b-ccc-discover.md` — delegated from step-02 for CCC-specific logic\n- `step-03b-context-snippet.md` — delegated from step-03 for snippet generation\n\n**New file:** `src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03c-component-extraction.md`\n\nStep-03 delegates to step-03c when `scope.type: \"component-library\"` is detected, then resumes at the extraction inventory (section 5).\n\n### Task 2.1: Component Extraction Sub-Step\n\n**File:** `src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03c-component-extraction.md` (NEW)\n\n**Content:** Four phases that replace the standard AST extraction flow:\n\n**Phase 1 — Registry Detection:**\n- If `scope.registry_path` specified in brief: load it directly\n- Otherwise: scan source tree for registry patterns:\n  - Files named `registry.ts`, `components.ts`, `index.ts` in `registry/`, `catalog/`, `components/` dirs\n  - Arrays of objects with `{ id, name, component }` structure\n  - Files with `Component[]` type annotations and 10+ entries\n- **Confidence scoring:** Rate each candidate (file size, entry count, field matches)\n- **User confirmation required:** Present top candidate(s) and ask user to confirm\n- Parse confirmed registry into `component_catalog[]`\n\n**Phase 2 — Props-First Extraction:**\n- Extract `export interface *Props` patterns first (primary API contracts)\n- For each Props interface: extract fields, types, defaults, optionality, JSDoc\n- Link Props to components via:\n  1. Naming convention (`FooProps` → `Foo`) — primary\n  2. File co-location (Props defined in same file as component) — fallback\n  3. Generic parameter (`ComponentProps<typeof Foo>`) — deep fallback\n- Extract `export function` patterns second — link to Props, don't document signatures\n- Extract shared types (same as standard flow)\n\n**Phase 3 — Variant Consolidation:**\n- If `scope.ui_variants` specified or multiple variant directories detected:\n  - Group components by registry `id` (not by filename — use registry as source of truth)\n  - For each group: use primary variant as canonical props definition\n  - Merge `availableIn[]` from all variants\n  - Flag props differences between variants as variant-specific notes\n- Output: deduplicated component list with `availableIn[]` metadata\n\n**Phase 4 — Demo Exclusion Application:**\n- Apply confirmed exclusion patterns from Phase 1 briefing (or auto-detected in extraction)\n- Report final counts: `{N} components, {M} demo files excluded, {K} variants consolidated`\n\n**Output:** Enriched `extraction_inventory` compatible with standard step-05 compilation, plus `component_catalog[]` in context.\n\n**Reference patterns:**\n- Copy step structure from `step-02b-ccc-discover.md` (delegation pattern)\n- Copy extraction inventory format from `step-03-extract.md` lines 182-210\n- Copy YAML rule recipe format from `extraction-patterns.md` lines 214-315\n\n### Task 2.2: Step-03 Delegation Gate\n\n**File:** `src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md`\n\n**Changes:**\n1. **After section 2 (Apply Scope Filters), before section 3** — Add delegation check:\n   ```markdown\n   ### 2b. Component Library Delegation\n\n   **If `scope.type: \"component-library\"` in the brief:**\n\n   \"**Component library detected.** Delegating to specialized extraction strategy.\"\n\n   Load and execute `./step-03c-component-extraction.md`. When complete, resume at section 5 (Build Extraction Inventory) with the enriched extraction data.\n\n   **Otherwise:** Continue with standard extraction below.\n   ```\n\n**Verification:** Read step-03 and confirm delegation is after scope filters but before AST extraction.\n\n### Task 2.3: Component-Specific Extraction Patterns\n\n**File:** `src/workflows/create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md`\n\n**Changes:**\n1. **New section after YAML Rule Recipes** — Add component-library-specific patterns:\n\n   **React/TSX component patterns:**\n   ```yaml\n   id: react-props-interfaces\n   language: typescript\n   rule:\n     pattern: 'export interface $NAME { $$$ }'\n   constraints:\n     NAME:\n       regex: '.*Props$'\n   ```\n\n   ```yaml\n   id: react-component-exports\n   language: tsx\n   rule:\n     pattern: 'export function $NAME($$$PARAMS) { $$$ }'\n   constraints:\n     NAME:\n       regex: '^[A-Z]'  # PascalCase = component convention\n   ```\n\n   **Vue SFC pattern:**\n   ```yaml\n   id: vue-define-props\n   language: typescript\n   rule:\n     pattern: 'defineProps<$TYPE>()'\n   ```\n\n**Reference:** Copy from existing YAML recipes at lines 214-315.\n\n### Task 2.4: Component-Library Compilation Rules\n\n**File:** `src/workflows/create-skill/data/compile-assembly-rules.md`\n\n**Changes:**\n1. **New conditional section** — Add component-library assembly overrides:\n\n   **Section 2 (Quick Start) override:**\n   - Add Props Reference format (for Tier 2 override):\n   ```markdown\n   ### {ComponentName}\n\n   **Install:** `npx {cli} add {component-id}`\n   **Available in:** {variant list}\n   **Props:** `{ComponentName}Props`\n\n   | Prop | Type | Default | Description |\n   |------|------|---------|-------------|\n   | {prop} | {type} | {default} | {description} |\n   ```\n\n### Task 2.6: Context Snippet Adaptation\n\n**File:** `src/workflows/create-skill/data/compile-assembly-rules.md` (or context-snippet section)\n\n**Changes:**\n1. 1	# Skill Brief Schema

3	## Required Fields
4	
5	| Field | Type | Constraint | Description |
6	|-------|------|------------|-------------|
7	| name | string | kebab-case `[a-z0-9-]+` | Unique skill identifier |
8	| version | string | Semantic version (`X.Y.Z` or `X.Y.Z-prerelease`) | Auto-detect from source (see Version Detection below), fall back to `1.0.0` |
9	| source_repo | string | GitHub URL or local path | Repository or project root (optional when `source_type: "docs-only"`) |
10	| language | string | Recognized language | Primary programming language |
11	| scope | object | See Scope Object below | Boundary definition |
12	| description | string | 1-3 sentences | What the skill covers |
13	| forge_tier | string | `Quick` / `Forge` / `Forge+` / `Deep` | Inherited from forge-tier.yaml (Title Case) |
14	| created | string | ISO date `YYYY-MM-DD` | Generation date |
15	| created_by | string | user_name from config | Who generated the brief |
16	
17	## Optional Fields
18	
19	| Field | Type | Constraint | Description |
20	|-------|------|------------|-------------|
21	| source_type | string | `source` or `docs-only` | Default `source`. When `docs-only`: `source_repo` optional, `doc_urls` required |
22	| doc_urls | array | `{url, label}` objects | Documentation URLs for T3 content. Required when `source_type: "docs-only"` |
23	| `scripts_intent` | string | `detect` / `none` / free-text | Describes whether scripts should be extracted. Values: `detect` (auto-detect from source — default when absent), `none` (skip scripts), or a free-text description of expected scripts (e.g., "CLI validation tools in bin/"). |
24	| `assets_intent` | string | `detect` / `none` / free-text | Describes whether assets should be extracted. Values: `detect` (auto-detect from source — default when absent), `none` (skip assets), or a free-text description of expected assets (e.g., "JSON schemas in schemas/"). |
25	| `source_authority` | string | `official` / `community` / `internal` | Default `community`. Set to `official` only when the skill creator is the library maintainer. Forced to `community` when `source_type: "docs-only"`. |
26	
27	When `source_type: "docs-only"`:
28	- `source_repo` becomes optional (set to doc site URL for reference)
29	- `doc_urls` must have at least one entry
30	- `source_authority` is forced to `community` (T3 external documentation cannot be `official`)
31	- All extracted content gets `[EXT:{url}]` citations

35	During brief creation, attempt to auto-detect the source version before defaulting to `"1.0.0"`. Check the first matching file in the source:
36	
37	- **Python:** `pyproject.toml` `[project] version` (static) → if `dynamic = ["version"]`, check `__init__.py` for `__version__` → `_version.py` if exists → `setup.py` `version=` → `git describe --tags --abbrev=0`
38	- **JavaScript/TypeScript:** `package.json` (`"version"`)
39	- **Rust:** `Cargo.toml` `[package] version` (static) → if `version = { workspace = true }`, resolve from workspace root `Cargo.toml` → `git describe --tags --abbrev=0`
40	- **Go:** version tag from `go.mod` or `git describe --tags --abbrev=0`
41	
42	If the source is a remote GitHub repo, use `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/{file}` to read the version file. If the source is local, read the file directly.
43	
44	If detection succeeds, use the detected version. If it fails or returns a non-semver value, fall back to `"1.0.0"`.
45	
46	The create-skill workflow (step-03-extract) also performs version reconciliation at extraction time — if the source version has changed since the brief was created, the extraction step warns and uses the source version.
47	
48	**Pre-release handling:** If the detected version contains a pre-release tag (e.g., `1.0.0-beta.0`, `2.0.0-rc.1`), preserve it as-is. Pre-release tags are valid semver and must not be stripped. When comparing versions during reconciliation, use semver-aware comparison that respects pre-release ordering.
49	
50	## Scope Object Structure
51	
52	```yaml
53	scope:
54	  type: full-library | specific-modules | public-api | docs-only
55	  include:
56	    - "src/**/*.ts"           # Glob patterns for included files/directories
57	  exclude:
58	    - "**/*.test.*"           # Glob patterns for excluded files
59	    - "**/node_modules/**"
60	  notes: "Optional notes about scope decisions"
61	```
62	
63	## YAML Template
64	
65	```yaml
66	---
67	name: "{skill-name}"
68	version: "{detected-version or 1.0.0}"  # Auto-detect from source, fall back to 1.0.0
69	source_type: "source"                    # "source" (default) or "docs-only"
70	source_repo: "{github-url-or-local-path}"
71	language: "{detected-language}"
72	description: "{brief-description}"
73	forge_tier: "{Quick|Forge|Forge+|Deep}"
74	created: "{date}"
75	created_by: "{user_name}"
76	scope:
77	  type: "{full-library|specific-modules|public-api|docs-only}"
78	  include:
79	    - "{pattern}"
80	  exclude:
81	    - "{pattern}"
82	  notes: "{optional-scope-notes}"
83	# Optional: documentation URLs for T3 content (required when source_type: "docs-only")
84	# doc_urls:
85	#   - url: "https://docs.example.com/api"
86	#     label: "API Reference"
87	# scripts_intent: detect         # Optional: detect | none | description
88	# assets_intent: detect          # Optional: detect | none | description
89	# source_authority: community    # Optional: official | community | internal
90	---
91	```
92	
93	## Human-Readable Presentation Format
94	
95	When presenting the brief for confirmation (brief-skill step 04 only — not applicable to analyze-source batch generation), display as:
96	
97	```
98	Skill Brief: {name}
99	====================
100	
101	Target:      {source_repo}
102	Language:    {language}
103	Forge Tier:  {forge_tier}
104	Description: {description}
105	
106	Scope: {scope.type}
107	  Include: {scope.include patterns, one per line}
108	  Exclude: {scope.exclude patterns, one per line}
109	  Notes:   {scope.notes}
110	
111	{If source_type is "docs-only":}
112	Source Type: docs-only
113	Doc URLs:
114	  {doc_urls, one per line with labels}
115	
116	{If source_type is "source" AND supplemental doc_urls collected:}
117	Supplemental Docs:
118	  {doc_urls, one per line with labels}
119	
120	{If scripts_intent or assets_intent was explicitly set (not default "detect"):}
121	Scripts:    {scripts_intent}
122	Assets:     {assets_intent}
123	
124	Source Authority: {source_authority}
125	
126	Version:    {version}
127	Created:    {created}
128	Created by: {created_by}
129	```
130	
131	## Validation Rules
132	
133	1. `name` must be unique within {forge_data_folder}
134	2. `source_repo` must be accessible (gh api for GitHub, path exists for local)
135	3. `language` must be a recognized programming language
136	4. `scope.type` must be one of the four defined types
137	5. `scope.include` must have at least one pattern (exception: `docs-only` scope, where include patterns are optional since no source code is available)
138	6. `forge_tier` must be one of: Quick, Forge, Forge+, Deep (Title Case, must match the tier from forge-tier.yaml, or default to Quick)
139	7. When `source_type: "docs-only"`: `doc_urls` must have >= 1 entry, `source_repo` becomes optional
140	8. Each `doc_urls` entry must have a valid `url` field
141	
```

1	# Scope Templates Reference
2	
3	## Scope Type Options
4	
5	Present these options to the user for selection:
6	
7	**[F] Full Library** — Include everything. Best for smaller, focused libraries.
8	- All public exports, all modules
9	- Exclude only tests, build artifacts, and internal utilities
10	
11	**[M] Specific Modules** — Select which modules to include. Best for large libraries where only some parts are relevant.
12	- You choose which modules/directories
13	- Fine-grained control over what's in and out
14	
15	**[P] Public API Only** — Include only the public-facing API surface. Best for libraries with a clear public/private boundary.
16	- Entry points and exported interfaces only
17	- Internal implementation excluded
18	
19	## Boundary Definitions by Scope Type
20	
21	### Full Library Boundaries
22	
23	Default inclusions:
24	- All source files under {main source directory}
25	- All public modules: {list from analysis}
26	
27	Default exclusions:
28	- Test files (`**/*.test.*`, `**/*.spec.*`, `**/test/`, `**/tests/`)
29	- Build artifacts (`**/dist/`, `**/build/`, `**/target/`)
30	- Configuration files
31	- Documentation source files
32	
33	Prompt: "Any additional exclusions you'd like to add? Or adjustments to these defaults?"
34	
35	### Specific Modules Boundaries
36	
37	**Phase 1 — Module selection:**
38	
39	Present numbered list of modules from step 02 with brief descriptions.
40	Prompt: "Which modules would you like to include? (Enter numbers, comma-separated):"
41	
42	**Phase 2 — Granularity within selected modules:**
43	
44	For selected modules, ask:
45	- **A)** Everything in those modules (all files)
46	- **B)** Only public exports from those modules
47	
48	Prompt: "Any files or patterns to explicitly exclude within these modules?"
49	
50	### Public API Only Boundaries
51	
52	**Phase 1 — Export selection:**
53	
54	Present numbered list of exports/entry points from step 02.
55	Prompt: "Which of these would you like to include? (Enter numbers, or 'all'):"
56	
57	**Phase 2 — Confirm exclusions:**
58	
59	Exclusions will include all internal implementation files, tests, and utilities.
60	Prompt: "Any additional items you'd like to include or exclude?"
61	
62	### Docs-Only Boundaries
63	
64	**No source code access.** Scope is defined by the `doc_urls` collected during intent gathering.
65	
66	- All content derived from external documentation
67	- No include/exclude patterns — coverage determined by fetched documentation
68	- All extractions labeled T3 (`[EXT:{url}]` citations)
69	
70	Prompt: "Any additional documentation URLs to include? Or URLs to exclude from the ones collected?"
71	
72	## Scripts & Assets Detection (Optional Refinement)
73	
74	When `scripts_intent` or `assets_intent` is `detect` (default), SKF auto-detects from source directories matching: `scripts/`, `bin/`, `tools/`, `cli/` (for scripts) and `assets/`, `templates/`, `schemas/`, `configs/`, `examples/` (for assets). Detection applies to all scope types except `docs-only`.

11	# Step 3: Scope Definition
12	
13	## STEP GOAL:
14	
15	To collaboratively define the skill's inclusion and exclusion boundaries using the analysis findings from step 02, scope templates, and the user's intent from step 01.
16	
17	## MANDATORY EXECUTION RULES (READ FIRST):
18

20

32	- ✅ We engage in collaborative dialogue, not command-response

34	- ✅ Maintain warm, guiding tone — help the user make informed scoping decisions
35	
36	### Step-Specific Rules:
37

39	- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to make scope decisions unilaterally — user drives all scope choices
40	- 💬 Approach: Intent-based discovery — "Which parts are most relevant to your use case?"
41	- 📋 Produce: scope type, include patterns, exclude patterns

45	- 🎯 Follow the MANDATORY SEQUENCE exactly
46	- 💾 Accumulate scope decisions conversationally for use in subsequent steps
47	- 📖 Reference analysis findings from step 02 throughout
48	- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to proceed without user confirmation of scope boundaries
49	
50	## CONTEXT BOUNDARIES:
51	
52	- Available context: Target repo, user intent, skill name, forge tier (step 01), repo structure, detected language, modules, exports (step 02)
53	- Focus: Defining WHAT is in scope and what is NOT
54	- Limits: Do not generate the brief — only define boundaries
55	- Dependencies: Analysis findings from step 02

57	## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
58	
59	**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise unless user explicitly requests a change.
60	
61	### 1. Present Scope Context
62

64	
65	Based on the analysis, here's what we're working with:
66

81	{numbered list of doc_urls with labels}
82

84	Any additional documentation URLs to add?"

86	Wait for confirmation. Then skip to section 5 (Summarize Scope Decisions) with:

91	**If `source_type: "source"` (default):** Continue to scope templates below.

171	- IF P: Invoke {partyModeSkill}, and when finished redisplay the menu

173	- IF Any other comments or queries: help user respond then [Redisplay Menu Options](#6-present-menu-options)

178	- ONLY proceed to next step when user selects 'C'
179	- After other menu items execution, return to this menu

181

1	# Unit Detection Heuristics
2	
3	## Purpose
4	
5	Rules for identifying discrete skillable units within a project. A "skillable unit" is a self-contained component with clear boundaries that can be documented as an independent skill.
6	
7	## Detection Signals
8	
9	### Strong Signals (High Confidence)
10	
11	| Signal | Description | Example |
12	|--------|-------------|---------|
13	| Independent package.json / Cargo.toml / go.mod | Unit has its own dependency manifest | `packages/auth/package.json` |
14	| Separate entry point | Unit has a main/index file | `services/api/src/index.ts` |
15	| Docker/service definition | Unit runs as an independent service | `docker-compose.yml` service entry |
16	| Distinct export surface | Unit exports a public API consumed by others | `src/lib/index.ts` with re-exports |
17	| Workspace member | Listed in root workspace configuration | `pnpm-workspace.yaml` packages |
18	
19	### Moderate Signals (Medium Confidence)
20	
21	| Signal | Description | Example |
22	|--------|-------------|---------|
23	| Directory depth boundary | Top-level directory with self-contained structure | `src/modules/payments/` |
24	| Naming convention | Follows organizational naming pattern | `@org/package-name` |
25	| Separate test suite | Has its own test directory or config | `packages/auth/__tests__/` |
26	| README.md presence | Has documentation at directory level | `libs/utils/README.md` |
27	| CI/CD pipeline reference | Referenced in build/deploy configuration | `.github/workflows/deploy-auth.yml` |
28	
29	### Weak Signals (Low Confidence — Require Corroboration)
30	
31	| Signal | Description | Example |
32	|--------|-------------|---------|
33	| Large directory | Many files in a subtree | 50+ files under one directory |
34	| Comment boundaries | Code comments marking sections | `// --- Auth Module ---` |
35	| Import clustering | Files that import primarily from each other | Tight import graph cluster |
36	
37	## Boundary Classification
38	
39	### Service Boundary
40	- Independent deployable unit
41	- Own process, port, or container
42	- Clear network interface (REST, gRPC, message queue)
43	- Scope type: `full-library`
44	
45	### Package Boundary
46	- Workspace member or independently versioned package
47	- Own dependency manifest
48	- Exports consumed by other packages
49	- Scope type: `full-library` or `specific-modules`
50	
51	### Module Boundary
52	- Logical grouping within a single package
53	- Shared namespace or directory structure
54	- Internal cohesion, external coupling through defined interface
55	- Scope type: `specific-modules` or `public-api`
56	
57	### Library Boundary
58	- Third-party dependency with significant project-specific usage patterns
59	- Custom wrappers, configurations, or integration code
60	- Scope type: `public-api`
61	
62	## Disqualification Rules
63	
64	Do NOT recommend as a skillable unit if:
65	
66	1. **Too small**: Fewer than 3 source files or 100 lines of code
67	2. **Generated code**: Auto-generated files (protobuf, GraphQL codegen, etc.)
68	3. **Pure configuration**: Only config files with no logic
69	4. **Test-only**: Test utilities with no production code
70	5. **Vendor/dependency**: Third-party code copied into project
71	6. **Already skilled**: Existing skill found in forge_data_folder (recommend update-skill instead)
72	
73	## Script/Asset Detection Signals
74	
75	During per-unit analysis, check for scripts and assets alongside code exports.
76	
77	**Script signals:**
78	
79	| Strength | Signal | Example |
80	|----------|--------|---------|
81	| Strong | Entry point in `package.json` `bin`, Cargo.toml `[[bin]]`, pyproject.toml `[project.scripts]` | `"bin": { "migrate": "scripts/migrate.js" }` |
82	| Strong | Shebang + executable file | `#!/usr/bin/env python` in `scripts/setup.py` |
83	| Moderate | File in `scripts/`, `bin/`, `tools/`, `cli/` directory | `scripts/validate.sh` |
84	| Moderate | CI/CD reference to script | `.github/workflows/test.yml` runs `scripts/test.sh` |
85	
86	**Asset signals:**
87	
88	| Strength | Signal | Example |
89	|----------|--------|---------|
90	| Strong | JSON Schema file with `$schema` key | `schemas/config.schema.json` |
91	| Strong | Config template with `.example` or `.template` extension | `config.yaml.example` |
92	| Moderate | File in `assets/`, `templates/`, `schemas/`, `configs/`, `examples/` directory | `templates/report.hbs` |
93	| Moderate | OpenAPI/GraphQL definition | `openapi.json`, `schema.graphql` |
94	
95	**Per-unit output:** Record `has_scripts: boolean`, `has_assets: boolean`, `script_files: string[]`, `asset_files: string[]`.
96	
97	**Disqualify:** Generated files (dist/, build/), vendored dependencies, IDE configs (.vscode/, .idea/), binary files (.so, .dll, .jar).
98	
99	## Stack Skill Candidate Detection
100	
101	Flag units as stack skill candidates when:
102	
103	1. **Co-import frequency**: Two or more units are imported together in 3+ files
104	2. **Integration adapter**: A unit exists primarily to bridge two other units
105	3. **Shared state**: Multiple units read/write to the same data store
106	4. **Orchestration layer**: A unit coordinates calls across multiple other units
107	
108	## Tier-Aware Scanning Depth
109	
110	| Forge Tier | Scanning Approach |
111	|------------|-------------------|
112	| Quick | File structure analysis: directory trees, manifest files, entry points, naming conventions |
113	| Forge | AST analysis: export surfaces, import graphs, dependency trees, type hierarchies |
114	| Forge+ | AST + CCC: semantic file pre-ranking before structural analysis, CCC signals for relevance scoring |
115	| Deep | AST + QMD: temporal evolution, refactoring patterns, semantic relationships, architectural drift |
116	
```

Generate recommendation cards for the new units and present them for confirmation. Then redisplay this menu.

1	# Extraction Patterns by Tier
2	
3	## Quick Tier (No AST)
4	
5	Source reading via gh_bridge — infer exports from file structure and content.
6	
7	### Strategy
8	1. `gh_bridge.list_tree(owner, repo, branch)` — map source structure
9	2. Identify entry points: index files, main exports, public modules
10	3. `gh_bridge.read_file(owner, repo, path)` — read each entry point
11	4. Extract: exported function names, parameter lists, return types (from signatures)
12	5. Infer types from JSDoc, docstrings, type annotations in source
13	
14	### Confidence
15	- All results: T1-low (source reading without structural verification)
16	- No co-import detection available
17	- No AST-backed line numbers
18	
19	### Supported Patterns
20	- `export function name(...)` / `export const name = ...` (JS/TS)
21	- `pub fn name(...)` (Rust)
22	- `def name(...)` with `__all__` (Python)
23	- `func Name(...)` (Go, capitalized = exported)
24	
25	---
26	
27	## Forge Tier (AST Available)
28	
29	Structural extraction via ast-grep — verified exports with line-level citations.
30	
31	> **Note:** `ast_bridge.*`, `qmd_bridge.*`, and `ccc_bridge.*` references below are **conceptual interfaces**, not callable functions. Resolve them as follows:
32	> - `ast_bridge.*` → ast-grep MCP tools (`mcp__ast-grep__find_code`, `mcp__ast-grep__find_code_by_rule`) or `ast-grep` CLI
33	> - `qmd_bridge.*` → QMD MCP tools (`mcp__plugin_qmd-plugin_qmd__search`, `vector_search`) or `qmd` CLI
34	> - `ccc_bridge.*` → `/ccc` skill (Claude Code), ccc MCP server (Cursor), or `ccc` CLI
35	> - `gh_bridge.*` → `gh api` commands or direct file I/O for local sources
36	>
37	> See [knowledge/tool-resolution.md](../../../knowledge/tool-resolution.md) for the complete resolution table. Also see the AST Extraction Protocol section below and the TOOL/SUBPROCESS FALLBACK rule for dispatch details.
38	
39	### Strategy
40	
41	1. Detect language from brief or file extensions
42	2. Use ast-grep to extract all exports from `path` for the given `language` (scan definitions)
43	3. For each export: function name, full signature, parameter types, return type, line number
44	4. Use ast-grep to detect co-imported symbols in `path` for the given `libraries[]`
45	5. Build extraction rules YAML for reproducibility
46	
47	### Confidence
48	- Exported functions with full signatures: T1 (AST-verified)
49	- Type definitions and interfaces: T1
50	- Co-import patterns: T1
51	- Internal/private functions: excluded (not part of public API)
52	
53	### ast-grep Patterns
54	- JS/TS: `export function $NAME($$$PARAMS): $RET` / `export const $NAME = ($$$PARAMS) => $BODY` / `export const $NAME` / `export class $NAME`
55	- Rust: `pub fn $NAME($$$PARAMS) -> $RET`
56	- Python: function definitions within `__all__` list
57	- Go: capitalized function definitions
58	
59	---
60	
61	## Forge+ Tier (AST + CCC)
62	
63	Identical extraction to Forge tier. CCC adds an upstream semantic discovery step that pre-ranks the file extraction queue.
64	
65	### When CCC Pre-Discovery Applies
66	
67	CCC pre-discovery runs in step-02b-ccc-discover (before this extraction step) when ALL of the following are true:
68	- Tier is Forge+ or Deep
69	- `tools.ccc: true` in forge-tier.yaml
70	- `ccc_index.status` is `"fresh"`, `"stale"`, `"created"`, or `"none"`/`"failed"` (step-02b attempts lazy indexing for the latter two)
71	
72	The discovery step stores `{ccc_discovery: [{file, score, snippet}]}` in context. This extraction step consumes those results to pre-rank the file list.
73	
74	### CCC Pre-Ranking Strategy
75	
76	When `{ccc_discovery}` is present and non-empty:
77	
78	1. Files appearing in `{ccc_discovery}` results move to the front of the extraction queue, sorted by relevance score descending
79	2. Files NOT in CCC results remain in the queue — they are not excluded, only deprioritized
80	3. If the CCC intersection with scoped files produces <10 files: include all scoped files (CCC results too narrow)
81	4. Proceed with the AST Extraction Protocol on the pre-ranked list
82	
83	### ast-grep Patterns
84	
85	Same patterns as Forge tier — see Forge tier section above. CCC pre-ranking does not change which AST patterns are used, only which files are processed first.
86	
87	### Confidence
88	
89	All results: T1 (AST-verified) — identical to Forge tier. CCC is upstream discovery only and is invisible in the output artifact.
90	
91	### Important
92	
93	CCC pre-discovery failures (ccc unavailable, command error, empty results) always result in standard Forge extraction behavior. This is not reported to the user as a problem — it is normal behavior when ccc has no relevant results for the skill's scope.
94	
95	---
96	
97	## Deep Tier (AST + QMD)
98	
99	Same extraction as Forge tier. Deep tier adds enrichment in step-04, not extraction.
100	
101	### Strategy
102	- Identical to Forge tier extraction
103	- QMD enrichment happens in the next step (step-04-enrich)
104	- Extraction results carry forward unchanged
105	
106	### Confidence
107	- Extraction: same as Forge (T1)
108	- Enrichment annotations added in step-04: T2
109	
110	---
111	
112	## AST Extraction Protocol
113	
114	When AST tools are available (Forge/Deep tier), follow this deterministic protocol to prevent output overflow on large codebases.
115	
116	**"Files in scope"** = files remaining after applying `include_patterns` and `exclude_patterns` from the brief, filtered by the target language extension. This is NOT the total repository file count from step-01's tree listing. Use the filtered count from step-03 section 2 as the decision tree input.
117	
118	### Decision Tree
119	
120	Apply the first matching condition:
121	
122	```
123	Files in scope ≤ 100
124	  → Use ast-grep MCP tool: find_code(pattern, max_results=100, output_format="text")
125	  → Parse compact text output directly into extraction inventory
126	
127	Files in scope 101–500
128	  → Use ast-grep MCP tool: find_code_by_rule(yaml, max_results=150, output_format="text")
129	  → Use scoped YAML rules (see recipes below) to filter at the AST level
130	  → Parse compact text output into extraction inventory
131	
132	Files in scope > 500
133	  → CLI streaming fallback: ast-grep run --json=stream + line-by-line Python processing
134	  → Process in directory batches, cap per-batch output
135	  → Merge batch results into extraction inventory
136	```
137	
138	### Safety Valve
139	
140	If any ast-grep operation (MCP or CLI) visibly causes a timeout, returns an error related to output size, or produces unexpectedly large output: immediately switch to the CLI streaming fallback with `--json=stream`. Do not retry the same approach. When falling back to the CLI streaming template, inject the brief's `scope.exclude` patterns into the `EXCLUDES` list (use `[]` if absent) — this applies regardless of which path triggered the fallback. Note: `max_results` in the MCP tool and `| head -N` in the CLI path provide hard caps, but this safety valve covers cases where the upstream tool itself fails before returning results (e.g., OOM during JSON serialization).
141	
142	### MCP Tool Usage (Preferred)
143	
144	**Simple pattern search:**
145	
146	```
147	find_code(
148	  project_folder="{source_path}",
149	  pattern="async def $NAME($$$PARAMS)",
150	  language="python",
151	  max_results=100,
152	  output_format="text"
153	)
154	```
155	
156	**Scoped YAML rule search (for larger repos):**
157	
158	```
159	find_code_by_rule(
160	  project_folder="{source_path}",
161	  yaml="id: public-api\nlanguage: python\nrule:\n  pattern: 'def $NAME($$$PARAMS)'\n  inside:\n    kind: module\n    stopBy: end\nconstraints:\n  NAME:\n    regex: '^[^_]'",
162	  max_results=150,
163	  output_format="text"
164	)
165	```
166	
167	### CLI Streaming Fallback
168	
169	When MCP tools are unavailable or the repo exceeds 500 files in scope, use `--json=stream` (NEVER `--json` or `--json=pretty`) with line-by-line Python processing:
170	
171	**Head cap selection:** The `| head -N` cap at the end of the pipeline controls how many exports are captured. Select `N` based on scope and tier:
172	- **Default (Quick/Forge, any scope):** `N = 200`
173	- **Forge+/Deep with `scope.type: "full-library"`:** `N = 500`
174	
175	For full-library skills at higher tiers, the larger cap prevents silently dropping internal module exports that maintainers need. The cap is applied AFTER exclude-pattern filtering, so useful results are not wasted on excluded files.
176	
177	```bash
178	# Note: use $$$ for variadic params in ast-grep patterns (e.g., 'def $NAME($$$PARAMS)')
179	# {exclude_patterns} = Python list from brief's scope.exclude, e.g. ['tests/**', '**/test_*']
180	# If scope.exclude is absent or empty in the brief, inject [] as the default.
181	# Patterns are matched against the full file path as emitted by ast-grep.
182	# Ensure paths are relative to the same root as the patterns (strip ./ prefix if needed).
183	# {HEAD_CAP} = 200 (default) or 500 (Forge+/Deep full-library) — see head cap selection above.
184	# IMPORTANT: The explicit 'run' subcommand is required for --json=stream to work.
185	ast-grep run -p '{pattern}' -l {language} --json=stream {path} | python3 -c "
186	import sys, json, fnmatch, signal
187	signal.signal(signal.SIGPIPE, signal.SIG_DFL)
188	
189	EXCLUDES = {exclude_patterns}
190	
191	for line in sys.stdin:
192	    try:
193	        m = json.loads(line)
194	        f = m.get('file','')
195	        if EXCLUDES and any(fnmatch.fnmatch(f, pat) for pat in EXCLUDES):
196	            continue
197	        v = m.get('metaVariables',{})
198	        name = v.get('single',{}).get('NAME',{}).get('text','')
199	        if name and not name.startswith('_'):
200	            ln = m.get('range',{}).get('start',{}).get('line',0)+1
201	            sig = m.get('text','').split(chr(10))[0].strip()
202	            print(f'[AST:{f}:L{ln}] {sig}')
203	    except: pass
204	" | head -{HEAD_CAP}
205	```
206	
207	**Critical constraints:**
208	
209	- ALWAYS use `--json=stream` — never `--json` (loads entire array into memory)
210	- ALWAYS process line-by-line (`for line in sys.stdin`) — never `json.load(sys.stdin)`
211	- ALWAYS cap output with `| head -N` as a safety valve
212	- For repos > 500 files, process in directory batches of 20-50 files each: split by top-level source directory, run the CLI streaming template per batch with the same head cap, then merge results and deduplicate by export name (keep the first occurrence if duplicates exist across batches)
213	
214	### YAML Rule Recipes by Language
215	
216	**Python — public functions:**
217	
218	```yaml
219	id: python-public-functions
220	language: python
221	rule:
222	  pattern: 'def $NAME($$$PARAMS)'
223	  inside:
224	    kind: module
225	    stopBy: end
226	constraints:
227	  NAME:
228	    regex: '^[^_]'
229	```
230	
231	**Python — public classes:**
232	
233	```yaml
234	id: python-public-classes
235	language: python
236	rule:
237	  pattern: 'class $NAME($$$BASES)'
238	  inside:
239	    kind: module
240	    stopBy: end
241	constraints:
242	  NAME:
243	    regex: '^[^_]'
244	```
245	
246	**JavaScript/TypeScript — exported functions:**
247	
248	> **Language selection:** Use `language: typescript` for `.ts` files and `language: tsx` for `.tsx` files. Patterns that work with `typescript` may return zero results with `tsx` and vice versa — they use different tree-sitter parsers. For mixed codebases, run each pattern twice (once per language) and merge results. Note: `export function` patterns may fail with `tsx` on ast-grep 0.41.x (see Known Limitations #5) — use source reading as fallback for those.
249	
250	```yaml
251	id: js-exported-functions
252	language: typescript  # Use 'tsx' for .tsx files — see language selection note above
253	rule:
254	  pattern: 'export function $NAME($$$PARAMS)'
255	```
256	
257	**JavaScript/TypeScript — exported constants:**
258	
259	```yaml
260	id: js-exported-constants
261	language: typescript
262	rule:
263	  pattern: 'export const $NAME = $VALUE'
264	```
265	
266	**JavaScript/TypeScript — exported arrow functions:**
267	
268	```yaml
269	id: js-exported-arrow-functions
270	language: typescript
271	rule:
272	  pattern: 'export const $NAME = ($$$PARAMS) => $BODY'
273	```
274	
275	> **JS/TS Pattern Merging:** Modern TypeScript codebases often use `export const` exclusively for all exports (arrow functions, objects, constants). Run ALL four JS/TS patterns (functions, arrow functions, constants, classes) and merge results by `$NAME`. Priority when deduplicating: arrow function match > function declaration match > constant match. Arrow function matches capture parameters directly; constant matches require inspecting `$VALUE` to extract signatures.
276	
277	**JavaScript/TypeScript — exported classes (use `find_code`, not `find_code_by_rule`):**
278	
279	```yaml
280	id: js-exported-classes
281	language: typescript
282	rule:
283	  pattern: 'export class $NAME'
284	```
285	
286	> **Important:** For class patterns, use `find_code()` rather than `find_code_by_rule()`. The `find_code_by_rule` API requires explicit AST `kind` rules for class exports, which adds complexity. The simpler `find_code()` pattern approach works reliably for class detection.
287	
288	**JavaScript/TypeScript — re-export detection (use `find_code`):**
289	
290	Use `find_code()` with pattern `export { $$$NAMES } from $SOURCE` for re-export detection. Note: this pattern may produce multiple AST node matches. Post-process results to split comma-separated names from `$$$NAMES`. For complex re-export chains (aliased exports, default re-exports, namespace re-exports), fall back to the Re-Export Tracing protocol in `extraction-patterns-tracing.md`.
291	
292	**Rust — public functions:**
293	
294	```yaml
295	id: rust-public-functions
296	language: rust
297	rule:
298	  any:
299	    - pattern: 'pub fn $NAME($$$PARAMS) -> $RET'
300	    - pattern: 'pub fn $NAME($$$PARAMS)'
301	```
302	
303	**Go — exported functions (capitalized):**
304	
305	```yaml
306	id: go-exported-functions
307	language: go
308	rule:
309	  any:
310	    - pattern: 'func $NAME($$$PARAMS) $RET'
311	    - pattern: 'func $NAME($$$PARAMS)'
312	constraints:
313	  NAME:
314	    regex: '^[A-Z]'
315	```
316	
317	### Known ast-grep Limitations
318	
319	When using ast-grep for extraction, be aware of these documented limitations:
320	
321	1. **`find_code_by_rule` requires explicit `kind` for class exports:** The `export class $NAME` pattern needs a `kind` rule specifying the tree-sitter node type when used with `find_code_by_rule`. Use the simpler `find_code()` API instead for class detection.
322	
323	2. **Re-export patterns produce multiple AST nodes:** `export { A, B, C } from './module'` decomposes into multiple metavariable bindings for `$$$NAMES`. Results require post-processing to split comma-separated names.
324	
325	3. **Default anonymous exports capture no name:** `export default function $NAME` works, but `export default $EXPR` (anonymous default export) captures no name in `$NAME`. Fall back to source reading (T1-low) for anonymous defaults.
326	
327	4. **Fallback protocol:** If an ast-grep pattern returns errors or zero results when results are expected:
328	   - First: retry with `find_code()` using a simpler pattern (drop type annotations, use broader match)
329	   - Second: if `find_code()` also fails, fall back to source reading for that pattern category (T1-low confidence)
330	   - Never silently accept zero results for a pattern category that the source language commonly uses
331	
332	5. **TSX `export function` pattern failure:** The `export function $NAME($$$PARAMS)` pattern may return zero results in TSX files with ast-grep 0.41.x. This affects both MCP tools and CLI. `export const` and `export type` patterns are unaffected. **Workaround:** For TSX files, use `export const` patterns first (which work), then fall back to source reading (grep/file read) for `export function` declarations. When a TSX codebase shows zero `export function` matches but source files clearly contain them, this is a known ast-grep tree-sitter tsx parser limitation — not an extraction error. Log it in the evidence report and proceed with T1-low confidence for those exports.
333	
334	6. **CLI `--json=stream` may produce no output:** On ast-grep 0.41.x, `--json=stream` may produce empty output for certain patterns. The `--json=stream` flag requires the explicit `run` subcommand: use `ast-grep run -p '{pattern}' --json=stream` (not `ast-grep -p '{pattern}' --json=stream`). If streaming still produces no output, fall back to the MCP tool or source reading.
335	
336	### Re-Export Tracing and Script/Asset Extraction
337	
338	See `extraction-patterns-tracing.md` for:
339	- **Re-export tracing protocol** — resolving module imports through `__init__.py`, barrel files, `pub use`
340	- **Script/asset extraction patterns** — detection heuristics, inclusion rules, provenance, inventory structure
341	
342	
```

Scan the scoped file tree for matching directories and files\n2. Count matches per pattern category\n3. Present to user: \"**Auto-detected {N} demo/example files** in {M} directories matching these patterns: {list}. Confirm exclusion? [Y/n] Or adjust patterns:\"\n4. Apply confirmed patterns to the exclude list before AST extraction\n5. Record in extraction inventory: `demo_files_excluded: {count}`\n\n### Re-Export Tracing and Script/Asset Extraction"
}
