```
50	The create-skill workflow (step-03-extract) also performs version reconciliation at extraction time — if the source version has changed since the brief was created, the extraction step warns and uses the source version.
51	
52	**Pre-release handling:** If the detected version contains a pre-release tag (e.g., `1.0.0-beta.0`, `2.0.0-rc.1`), preserve it as-is. Pre-release tags are valid semver and must not be stripped. When comparing versions during reconciliation, use semver-aware comparison that respects pre-release ordering.
53	
54	## Scope Object
55	
56	```yaml
57	scope:
58	  type: full-library | specific-modules | public-api | docs-only
59	  include:
60	    - 'src/**/*.ts'          # At least one required
61	  exclude:
62	    - 'src/**/*.test.ts'     # Optional
63	  notes: 'Optional rationale for scope decision'
64	```
65	
66	### Scope Types
67	
68	| Type | Use When |
69	|------|----------|
70	| full-library | Entire codebase of a unit |
71	| specific-modules | Selected components or packages |
72	| public-api | Only exported interfaces |
73	| docs-only | When source_type is docs-only — no source code available, all content from doc_urls |
74	
75	## YAML Template
76	
77	```yaml
78	name: '{unit-name}'
79	version: '{detected-version or 1.0.0}'
80	source_type: 'source'
81	source_repo: '{project_path}'
82	language: '{detected_language}'
83	scope:
84	  type: '{detected_scope_type}'
85	  include:
86	    - '{include_pattern}'
87	  exclude:
88	    - '{exclude_pattern}'
89	  notes: '{scope_rationale}'
90	description: '{1-3 sentence description}'
91	forge_tier: '{Quick|Forge|Forge+|Deep}'
92	created: '{current_date}'
93	created_by: '{user_name}'
94	```
95	
96	## Validation Rules
97	
98	1. **Name uniqueness**: No duplicate names within forge_data_folder
99	2. **Source accessibility**: source_repo path must exist or be reachable
100	3. **Language recognized**: Must be a known programming language
101	4. **Scope type valid**: Must match one of the four defined types (full-library, specific-modules, public-api, docs-only)
102	5. **Include patterns**: At least one include glob pattern required (exception: `docs-only` scope, where include patterns are optional since no source code is available)
103	6. **Forge tier match**: Must match value in forge-tier.yaml
104	7. **Docs-only mode**: When `source_type: "docs-only"`, `doc_urls` required (>= 1), `source_repo` optional
105	8. Each `doc_urls` entry must have a valid `url` field
106	
107	## Output Location
108	
109	Each skill-brief.yaml is written to:
```

+### Component Library Boundaries
+
+**Phase 1 — Registry Detection:**
+
+Auto-detect or accept explicit `registry_path` from user. Scan source tree for files matching common registry patterns:
+- Files named `registry.ts`, `components.ts`, `index.ts` in `registry/`, `catalog/`, or `components/` directories
+- Arrays of objects with `{ id, name, component }` structure and 10+ entries
+- Files with `Component[]` type annotations
+
+Present detected registry candidate(s) to user for confirmation.
+Prompt: "I found what looks like a component registry at {path} ({count} entries). Is this correct? Or provide the registry path:"
+
+**Phase 2 — Demo/Example Exclusion:**
+
+Auto-detect demo directories and file patterns:
+- Directories: `demo/`, `demos/`, `stories/`, `examples/`, `__stories__/`, `storybook/`
+- Files: `*.stories.*`, `*.story.*`, `*.example.*`, `*.demo.*`
+
+Present detected patterns to user before applying (do NOT exclude silently).
+Prompt: "**Auto-detected {N} demo/example files** in {M} directories. Confirm exclusion? [Y/n] Or adjust patterns:"
+
+**Phase 3 — Variant Selection (if applicable):**
+
+If multiple design system variant directories detected (e.g., `react-shadcn/`, `react-baseui/`, `react-carbon/`):
+- Present detected variants with component counts per variant
+- User selects primary variant and which variants to include
+- Record as `ui_variants` in brief
+
+Prompt: "I detected {count} design system variants: {list with counts}. Which is the primary variant? Include all? [Y/n]"
+
+**Phase 4 — Scope Confirmation:**
+
+Summary showing: component count, excluded demo count, variant summary, include/exclude patterns.
+Prompt: "Does this component library scope look right? Adjust before continuing."
+
 ## Scripts & Assets Detection (Optional Refinement)
 
 When `scripts_intent` or `assets_intent` is `detect` (default), SKF auto-detects from source directories matching: `scripts/`, `bin/`, `tools/`, `cli/` (for scripts) and `assets/`, `templates/`, `schemas/`, `configs/`, `examples/` (for assets). Detection applies to all scope types except `docs-only`.

   include:
     - "{pattern}"
   exclude:
@@ -133,7 +141,7 @@ Created by: {created_by}
 1. `name` must be unique within {forge_data_folder}
 2. `source_repo` must be accessible (gh api for GitHub, path exists for local)
 3. `language` must be a recognized programming language
-4. `scope.type` must be one of the four defined types
+4. `scope.type` must be one of the five defined types

 6. `forge_tier` must be one of: Quick, Forge, Forge+, Deep (Title Case, must match the tier from forge-tier.yaml, or default to Quick)
 7. When `source_type: "docs-only"`: `doc_urls` must have >= 1 entry, `source_repo` becomes optional

@@ -171,6 +171,7 @@ When MCP tools are unavailable or the repo exceeds 500 files in scope, use `--js
 **Head cap selection:** The `| head -N` cap at the end of the pipeline controls how many exports are captured. Select `N` based on scope and tier:
 - **Default (Quick/Forge, any scope):** `N = 200`
 - **Forge+/Deep with `scope.type: "full-library"`:** `N = 500`
+- **Forge+/Deep with `scope.type: "component-library"`:** `N = 300` (components have fewer but richer exports; props interfaces are the primary API surface)
 
 For full-library skills at higher tiers, the larger cap prevents silently dropping internal module exports that maintainers need. The cap is applied AFTER exclude-pattern filtering, so useful results are not wasted on excluded files.
 
@@ -333,6 +334,28 @@ When using ast-grep for extraction, be aware of these documented limitations:
 
 6. **CLI `--json=stream` may produce no output:** On ast-grep 0.41.x, `--json=stream` may produce empty output for certain patterns. The `--json=stream` flag requires the explicit `run` subcommand: use `ast-grep run -p '{pattern}' --json=stream` (not `ast-grep -p '{pattern}' --json=stream`). If streaming still produces no output, fall back to the MCP tool or source reading.
 
+### Component Library Demo/Example Auto-Exclusion
+
+When `scope.type: "component-library"`, auto-detect and propose demo/example exclusions before extraction begins. **User confirmation is required before applying** — some `examples/` directories contain API-level code.
+
+**Auto-detect directory patterns:**
+- `**/demo/**`, `**/demos/**`
+- `**/stories/**`, `**/__stories__/**`, `**/storybook/**`
+- `**/examples/**`, `**/example/**`
+
+**Auto-detect file patterns:**
+- `**/*.stories.*`, `**/*.story.*`
+- `**/*.example.*`, `**/*.demo.*`
+
+If `demo_patterns` is specified in the brief, use those instead of auto-detection.
+
+**Procedure:**
+1. Scan the scoped file tree for matching directories and files
+2. Count matches per pattern category
+3. Present to user: "**Auto-detected {N} demo/example files** in {M} directories matching these patterns: {list}. Confirm exclusion? [Y/n] Or adjust patterns:"
+4. Apply confirmed patterns to the exclude list before AST extraction
+5. Record in extraction inventory: `demo_files_excluded: {count}`
+
 ### Re-Export Tracing and Script/Asset Extraction
 
 See `extraction-patterns-tracing.md` for:

1	---
2	name: 'step-03-extract'
3	description: 'Tier-dependent source code extraction — AST or source reading for exports, signatures, and types'

9	---
10	
11	# Step 3: Extract
12	
13	## STEP GOAL:
14	
15	To extract all public exports, function signatures, type definitions, and co-import patterns from the source code using tier-appropriate tools, building a complete extraction inventory with confidence-tiered provenance citations.
16	
17	## MANDATORY EXECUTION RULES (READ FIRST):
18	
19	### Universal Rules:
20	
21	- 📖 CRITICAL: Read the complete step file before taking any action
22	- 🎯 ALWAYS follow the exact instructions in the step file
23	- ⚙️ TOOL/SUBPROCESS FALLBACK: If any instruction references a tool you do not have access to, you MUST still achieve the outcome in your main context thread
24	- ✅ YOU MUST ALWAYS SPEAK OUTPUT in your Agent communication style with the config `{communication_language}`
25	
26	### Role Reinforcement:
27	
28	- ✅ You are a skill compilation engine performing structural extraction
29	- ✅ Zero hallucination tolerance — every extracted item must trace to source code
30	- ✅ Confidence tier labeling is mandatory for every extraction result
31	
32	### Step-Specific Rules:
33	
34	- 🎯 Focus ONLY on extracting exports, signatures, types from source code
35	- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to compile SKILL.md content — that's step-05
36	- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to write any output files — extraction stays in context
37	- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to include content that cannot be cited to a source location
38	- ⚒️ Every extracted item MUST have a provenance citation: `[AST:{file}:L{line}]` or `[SRC:{file}:L{line}]`
39	
40	## EXECUTION PROTOCOLS:
41	
42	- 🎯 Follow MANDATORY SEQUENCE exactly
43	- 💾 Build extraction inventory in context — do not write files
44	- 📖 Load extraction patterns data file for tier-specific strategy
45	- 🚫 If an export cannot be verified, exclude it — do not guess
46	
47	## CONTEXT BOUNDARIES:
48	
49	- Available: brief_data, tier, source_root, file_tree from step-01; ecosystem check outcome from step-02 (proceed/halt decision — no named variable stored)
50	- Focus: Source code extraction and inventory building
51	- Limits: Do NOT compile, assemble, or write any output
52	- Dependencies: Source code must be accessible (resolved in step-01)
53	
54	## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
55	
56	**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise.
57	
58	### 1. Load Extraction Patterns
59	
60	Load `{extractionPatternsData}` completely. Identify the strategy for the current forge tier.
61	
62	### 2. Apply Scope Filters
63	
64	From the brief, apply scope and pattern filters:
65	
66	- `scope` — determines what to extract (e.g., "all public exports", specific modules)
67	- `include_patterns` — file globs to include (if specified)
68	- `exclude_patterns` — file globs to exclude (if specified)
69	
70	Build the filtered file list from the source tree resolved in step-01. Record the result: "**Filtered file count: {N} files in scope**" — this count is the input to the AST Extraction Protocol decision tree in the extraction patterns data file.
71	
72	### 3. Check for Docs-Only Mode
73	
74	**If `source_type: "docs-only"` in the brief data:**
75	
76	"**Docs-only mode:** No source code to extract. Documentation content will be fetched from `doc_urls` in step-03c."
77	
78	Build an empty extraction inventory with zero exports. Set `extraction_mode: "docs-only"` in context. Auto-proceed through Gate 2 (section 6) — display the empty inventory and note that T3 content will be produced by the doc-fetcher step.
79	
80	**If `source_type: "source"` (default):** Continue with extraction below.
81	
82	### 4. Execute Tier-Dependent Extraction
83	
84	Load `{sourceResolutionData}` completely. Follow the **Remote Source Resolution** protocol for Forge/Deep tiers (ephemeral clone, sparse-checkout, cleanup) and the **Version Reconciliation** protocol for all tiers. Apply the protocols before proceeding with the tier-specific extraction strategy below.
85	
86	**Quick Tier (No AST tools):**
87	
88	1. Use `gh_bridge.list_tree(owner, repo, branch)` to map source structure (if remote)
89	2. Identify entry points: index files, main exports, public modules
90	3. Use `gh_bridge.read_file(owner, repo, path)` to read each entry point
91	4. Extract from source text: exported function names, parameter lists, return types
92	5. Infer types from JSDoc, docstrings, type annotations
93	6. Confidence: All results T1-low — `[SRC:{file}:L{line}]`
94	
95	**Tool resolution for gh_bridge:** Use `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/git/trees/{branch}?recursive=1` for list_tree, `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/{path}` for read_file. If source is local, use direct file listing/reading instead. See [knowledge/tool-resolution.md](../../../knowledge/tool-resolution.md).
96	
97	**Forge/Forge+/Deep Tier (AST available):**
98	
99	**Deferred CCC Discovery (Forge+ and Deep — remote sources only):**
100	
101	If ALL of these conditions are true:
102	- `tools.ccc` is true in forge-tier.yaml
103	- `{ccc_discovery}` is empty (step-02b deferred because source was remote)
104	- `ephemeral_clone_active` is true (clone succeeded in source resolution above)
105	- Tier is Forge+ or Deep
106	
107	Then run CCC indexing and discovery on the ephemeral clone:
108	
109	1. **Index the clone:** Run `cd {temp_path} && ccc init` then `ccc index` with an extended timeout or in background mode — `ccc init` takes no positional arguments and initializes the index for the current working directory. Indexing can take several minutes on large codebases (1000+ files). Use `ccc status` to verify completion — check that `Chunks` and `Files` counts are non-zero. If init fails or indexing fails, set `{ccc_discovery: []}` and continue — this is not an error.
110	
111	2. **Construct semantic query:** Build from brief data: `"{brief.name} {brief.scope}"`. Truncate to 80 characters — keep the full skill name and trim `brief.scope` from the end. If `brief.scope` is very short (< 10 chars), append terms from `brief.description` to fill the remaining space.
112	
113	3. **Execute search:** Run `ccc_bridge.search(query, temp_path, top_k=20)`:
114	   - **Tool resolution:** Use `/ccc` skill search (Claude Code), ccc MCP server (Cursor), or `cd {temp_path} && ccc search --limit 20 "{query}"` (CLI). Note: `ccc search` operates on the index in the current working directory. See [knowledge/tool-resolution.md](../../../knowledge/tool-resolution.md).
115	
116	4. **Store results:** If search succeeds, store as `{ccc_discovery: [{file, score, snippet}]}`. Display: "**CCC semantic discovery (post-clone): {N} relevant regions identified across {M} unique files.**"
117	
118	5. **On failure:** Set `{ccc_discovery: []}`. Display: "CCC post-clone discovery unavailable — proceeding with standard extraction." Do NOT halt.
119	
120	**CCC Discovery Integration (Forge+ and Deep with ccc only):**
121	
122	If `{ccc_discovery}` is in context and non-empty (populated by step-02b or deferred discovery above):
123	- Sort the filtered file list by CCC relevance score: files appearing in `{ccc_discovery}` results move to the front of the extraction queue, sorted by their relevance score descending
124	- Files NOT in CCC results remain in the queue after ranked files — they are not excluded, only deprioritized
125	- Display: "**CCC discovery: {N} files pre-ranked by semantic relevance** — extraction will prioritize these first."
126	
127	If `{ccc_discovery}` is empty or not in context: proceed with existing file ordering (no change to current behavior).
128	
129	⚠️ **CRITICAL:** Before executing AST extraction, load the **AST Extraction Protocol** section from `{extractionPatternsData}`. Follow the decision tree based on the file count from step-01's file tree. This determines whether to use the MCP tool, scoped YAML rules, or CLI streaming. Never use `ast-grep --json` (without `=stream`) — it loads the entire result set into memory and will fail on large codebases. Always use the explicit `run` subcommand with streaming: `ast-grep run -p '{pattern}' --json=stream`.
130	
131	1. Detect language from brief or file extensions
132	2. Follow the AST Extraction Protocol decision tree from `{extractionPatternsData}`:
133	   - ≤100 files: use `find_code()` MCP tool with `max_results` and `output_format="text"`
134	   - ≤500 files: use `find_code_by_rule()` MCP tool with scoped YAML rules
135	   - >500 files: use CLI `--json=stream` with line-by-line streaming Python — **CRITICAL:** inject the brief's `scope.exclude` patterns into the Python filter's `EXCLUDES` list (use `[]` if absent) so excluded files are discarded before consuming `head -N` slots (see template in extraction patterns data)
136	3. For each export: extract function name, full signature, parameter types, return type, line number
137	4. Use `ast_bridge.detect_co_imports(path, libraries[])` to find integration points
138	5. Build extraction rules YAML data for reproducibility
139	6. Confidence: All results T1 — `[AST:{file}:L{line}]`
140	
141	**Tool resolution for ast_bridge:** Use ast-grep MCP tools (`mcp__ast-grep__find_code`, `mcp__ast-grep__find_code_by_rule`) as specified in the AST Extraction Protocol above, or `ast-grep` CLI. For `detect_co_imports`, use `find_code_by_rule` with a co-import YAML rule scoped to the libraries list. See [knowledge/tool-resolution.md](../../../knowledge/tool-resolution.md).
142	
143	**If AST tool is unavailable at Forge/Deep tier** (see `{tierDegradationRulesData}` for full rules):
144	
145	⚠️ **Warn the user explicitly:** "AST tools are unavailable — extraction will use source reading (T1-low). Run [SF] Setup Forge to detect and configure AST tools for T1 confidence."
146	
147	Degrade to Quick tier extraction. Note the degradation reason in context for the evidence report.
148	
149	**For each file — handle failures gracefully:**
150	
151	- If a file cannot be read: log warning, skip file, continue with remaining files
152	- If AST parsing fails on a file: fall back to source reading for that file, continue
153	
154	**Re-export tracing (Forge/Deep only):** After the initial AST scan, check for unresolved public exports from entry points (`__init__.py`, `index.ts`, `lib.rs`). Follow the **Re-Export Tracing** protocol in `{extractionPatternsTracingData}` to resolve them to their definition files.
155	
156	### 4b. Validate Exports Against Package Entry Point
157	
158	After extraction, validate the collected exports against the package's actual public API surface:
159	
160	- **Python:** Read `{source_root}/__init__.py` — extract imports to build the public export list. Compare against AST results:
161	  - In AST but not entry point → mark as internal (exclude from `metadata.json` exports)
162	  - In entry point but not AST → flag as extraction gap (trace via re-export protocol)
163	- **TypeScript/JS:** Read `index.ts`/`index.js` — same comparison logic.
164	- **Rust:** Read `lib.rs` — extract `pub use` items. Same logic. **Go:** Scan for exported (capitalized) identifiers.
165	
166	Use the entry point as the authoritative source for `metadata.json`'s `exports[]` array.
167	
168	**If entry point is missing or unreadable:** Skip validation with a warning.
169	
170	### 4c. Detect and Inventory Scripts/Assets
171	
172	**If `scripts_intent: "none"` AND `assets_intent: "none"` in brief:** Skip this section.
173	
174	After export extraction, scan the source for scripts and assets using the detection patterns in `{extractionPatternsTracingData}`:
175	
176	1. Scan source tree for directories/files matching detection heuristics (scripts/, bin/, tools/, cli/ for scripts; assets/, templates/, schemas/, configs/, examples/ for assets)
177	2. For each candidate: verify existence, check size (flag >500 lines), exclude binaries, compute SHA-256 hash
178	3. Extract purpose from header comments, shebang, README references, or schema fields. Record: file_path, purpose, source_path, language/type, content_hash, confidence (T1-low)
179	
180	Add results to `scripts_inventory[]` and `assets_inventory[]` alongside the existing export inventory.
181	
182	### 5. Build Extraction Inventory
183	
184	Compile all extracted data into a structured inventory:
185	
186	**Per-export entry:**
187	- Function/type name
188	- Full signature with types
189	- Parameters (name, type, required/optional)
190	- Return type
191	- Source file and line number
192	- Provenance citation (`[AST:...]` or `[SRC:...]`)
193	- Confidence tier (T1 or T1-low)
194	
195	**Aggregate counts:**
196	- Total files scanned
197	- Total exports found
198	- Exports by type (functions, types/interfaces, constants)
199	- Confidence breakdown (T1 count, T1-low count)
200	- `top_exports[]` — sorted list of the top 10-20 public API function names by prominence (import frequency or documentation position). This named field is consumed by step-03b for targeted temporal fetching and cache fingerprinting.
201	
202	**Script/asset counts (when detected):**
203	- `scripts_found`: count of scripts detected
204	- `assets_found`: count of assets detected
205	
206	**Co-import patterns (Forge/Deep only):**
207	- Libraries commonly imported alongside extracted exports
208	- Integration point suggestions
209	
210	### 6. Present Extraction Summary (Gate 2)
211	
212	Display the extraction findings for user confirmation:
213	
214	"**Extraction complete.**
215	
216	**Files scanned:** {file_count}
217	**Exports found:** {export_count} ({function_count} functions, {type_count} types, {constant_count} constants)
218	**Confidence:** {t1_count} T1 (AST-verified), {t1_low_count} T1-low (source reading)
219	**Tier used:** {tier}
220	**Co-import patterns:** {pattern_count} detected
221	{if scripts_found > 0: **Scripts detected:** {scripts_found}}
222	{if assets_found > 0: **Assets detected:** {assets_found}}
223	
224	**Top exports:**
225	{list top 10 exports with signatures}
226	
227	{warnings if any files skipped or degraded}
228	
229	Review the extraction summary above. Select an option to continue."
230	
231	### 7. Present MENU OPTIONS
232	
233	Display: "**Extraction Summary — Select an Option:** [C] Continue to compilation"
234	
235	#### EXECUTION RULES:
236	
237	- IF docs-only mode (`extraction_mode: "docs-only"`): Auto-proceed immediately to `{nextStepFile}` — no user interaction required
238	- OTHERWISE: ALWAYS halt and wait for user input after presenting the extraction summary
239	- This is Gate 2 — user must confirm before compilation proceeds (except docs-only mode)
240	- User may ask questions about the extraction results before continuing
241	
242	#### Menu Handling Logic:
243	
244	- IF C: Confirm extraction inventory is complete. Immediately load, read entire file, then execute `{nextStepFile}`
245	- IF Any other comments or queries: answer questions about the extraction results, then redisplay the menu
246	
247	## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE
248	
249	ONLY WHEN the extraction inventory is built with provenance citations and the user has confirmed the extraction summary will you proceed to load `{nextStepFile}` for temporal context fetching.
250	
251	---
252	
253	## 🚨 SYSTEM SUCCESS/FAILURE METRICS
254	
255	### ✅ SUCCESS:
256	
257	- Extraction patterns loaded and tier-appropriate strategy applied
258	- Scope filters applied from brief
259	- All accessible source files in scope scanned
260	- Every extracted item has a provenance citation with confidence tier
261	- Extraction inventory built with aggregate counts
262	- Graceful degradation if AST tools unavailable
263	- Gate 2 summary presented with export counts and confidence breakdown
264	- User confirmed before proceeding
265	
266	### ❌ SYSTEM FAILURE:
267	
268	- Including exports without provenance citations
269	- Guessing or hallucinating function signatures not in source
270	- Not applying scope/include/exclude filters from the brief
271	- Halting on individual file read failures instead of skipping
272	- Not presenting Gate 2 summary for user confirmation
273	- Beginning compilation or SKILL.md assembly in this step
274	- Not reporting confidence tier breakdown
275	
276	**Master Rule:** Zero hallucination — every extraction must trace to source code. Uncitable content is excluded, not guessed.
277	
```

1	# SKILL.md Section Structure
2	
3	## agentskills.io Compliant Format
4	
5	### Frontmatter (Required — agentskills.io Compliance)
6	
7	```yaml
8	---
9	name: {skill-name}
10	description: >
11	  {Trigger-optimized description of what the skill does and when to use it.
12	  Include specific keywords for agent discovery.
13	  Mention what NOT to use it for if applicable.
14	  1-1024 characters.}
15	---
16	```
17	
18	**Frontmatter rules (agentskills.io specification):**
19	
20	- `name`: 1-64 characters, lowercase alphanumeric + hyphens only, must match parent directory name. Prefer gerund form (`processing-pdfs`, `analyzing-spreadsheets`) for clarity; noun phrases and action-oriented forms are acceptable alternatives.
21	- `description`: 1-1024 characters, trigger-optimized for agent matching. MUST use third-person voice ("Processes..." not "I can..." or "You can...").
22	- Only 6 fields permitted: `name`, `description`, `license`, `compatibility`, `metadata`, `allowed-tools`
23	- `version` and `author` belong in metadata.json, NOT in frontmatter
24	
25	### Two-Tier Section Structure
26	
27	SKILL.md uses a two-tier structure to ensure actionable content survives `split-body` extraction.
28	
29	#### Tier 1 — Always Inline (survives split-body, target <300 lines)
30	
31	| # | Section | Budget | Purpose |
32	|---|---------|--------|---------|
33	| 1 | **Overview** | ~10 lines | What the library does, source repo, version, tier, export count |
34	| 2 | **Quick Start** | ~30 lines | 3-5 core functions with one runnable end-to-end example |
35	| 3 | **Common Workflows** | ~30 lines | 4-5 typical function call sequences for common tasks |
36	| 4 | **Key API Summary** | ~20 lines | Table of top 10-15 functions (name, purpose, key params) |
37	| 4b | **Migration & Deprecation Warnings** | ~10 lines | T2-future warnings inline (Deep tier only, skip if none) |
38	| 5 | **Key Types** | ~20 lines | Most important enum/type values inline |
39	| 6 | **Architecture at a Glance** | ~10 lines | Bullet list of subsystem categories |
40	| 7 | **CLI** | ~10 lines | Basic CLI commands (skip if no CLI) |
41	| 7b | **Scripts & Assets** | ~10 lines | Manifest of included scripts and assets (skip if none detected) |
42	| 8 | **Manual Sections** | ~5 lines | `<!-- [MANUAL] -->` markers for update-skill |
43	
44	#### Tier 2 — Reference-Eligible (extracted by split-body into references/)
45	
46	| # | Section | Purpose |
47	|---|---------|---------|
48	| 9 | **Full API Reference** | Complete signatures, parameter tables, return types, examples, citations |
49	| 10 | **Full Type Definitions** | All types, interfaces, enums with full field details |
50	| 11 | **Full Integration Patterns** | Co-import patterns, adapter details, pipeline internals (Forge/Deep only) |
51	
52	#### Assembly Rules
53	
54	- Tier 1 sections are assembled first — they form the standalone body
55	- Tier 2 sections are assembled after — they are progressive disclosure detail
56	- Tier 1 sections are kept short enough that `split-body` targets the larger Tier 2 sections (`## Full` headings) into `references/`
57	- After split-body, SKILL.md retains all Tier 1 content — actionable without loading references
58	- An agent loading only SKILL.md (no references) must get enough to act
59	- **Section 4b (Migration & Deprecation Warnings)** is conditional: only emitted for Deep tier when T2-future annotations exist. Quick/Forge/Forge+ tiers and Deep tiers without T2-future annotations omit it entirely (no empty section). Parsers and validators must treat this section as optional.
60	- **Section 7b (Scripts & Assets)** is conditional: only emitted when `scripts_inventory` or `assets_inventory` is non-empty. Omitted entirely when no scripts or assets are detected. Parsers and validators must treat this section as optional.
61	
62	### Provenance Citation Format
63	
64	| Tier | Format | Example |
65	|------|--------|---------|
66	| T1 (AST) | `[AST:{file}:L{line}]` | `[AST:src/auth/index.ts:L42]` |
67	| T1-low (Source) | `[SRC:{file}:L{line}]` | `[SRC:src/auth/index.ts:L42]` |
68	| T2 (QMD) | `[QMD:{collection}:{doc}]` | `[QMD:project:CHANGELOG.md]` |
69	| T3 (External) | `[EXT:{url}]` | `[EXT:docs.example.com/api]` |
70	
71	### [MANUAL] Section Markers
72	
73	Seed empty manual sections for future update-skill compatibility:
74	
75	```markdown
76	<!-- [MANUAL:additional-notes] -->
77	<!-- Add custom notes here. This section is preserved during skill updates. -->
78	<!-- [/MANUAL:additional-notes] -->
79	```
80	
81	Place after Quick Start and after API Reference sections.
82	
83	---
84	
85	## context-snippet.md Format (Vercel-aligned indexed format)
86	
87	Indexed pipe-delimited format for CLAUDE.md managed section (~80-120 tokens per skill). Aligned with Vercel's research finding that retrieval instructions + indexed file maps + inline gotchas dramatically improve agent performance.
88	
89	```markdown
90	[{skill-name} v{version}]|root: skills/{skill-name}/
91	|IMPORTANT: {skill-name} v{version} — read SKILL.md before writing {skill-name} code. Do NOT rely on training data.
92	|quick-start:{SKILL.md#quick-start}
93	|api: {top exports with () for functions, comma-separated}
94	|key-types:{SKILL.md#key-types} — {inline summary of most important type values}
95	|gotchas: {2-3 most critical pitfalls or breaking changes, inline}
96	```
97	
98	### Format rules
99	
100	- **Line 1:** Skill name + version + root path — version signals training data staleness
101	- **Line 2 (IMPORTANT):** Retrieval instruction — always present, tells agent to read SKILL.md before acting
102	- **Lines 3+:** Pipe-delimited index mapping topics to SKILL.md sections (with `#anchor` pointers)
103	- **Inline content:** Each index line includes a brief inline summary (~10 words) so the agent can decide whether to read the full section
104	- **gotchas line:** 2-3 most critical pitfalls inline — prevents mistakes without requiring a file read
105	- **Token budget:** ~80-120 tokens per skill (justified by Vercel's finding that indexed format maintains performance at 80% compression)
106	- **T1-now content only** — no T2 annotations in the snippet
107	- **Section anchors** (`#quick-start`, `#key-types`) must match actual SKILL.md heading slugs
108	- **Version** comes from source detection (per issue #32), not brief default
109	
110	---
111	
112	## metadata.json Structure
113	
114	```json
115	{
116	  "name": "{skill-name}",
117	  "version": "{source-version}",
118	  "skill_type": "single",
119	  "source_authority": "{official|community|internal}",
120	  "source_repo": "{github-url}",
121	  "source_root": "{resolved-source-path}",
122	  "source_commit": "{commit-hash}",
123	  "confidence_tier": "{Quick|Forge|Forge+|Deep}",
124	  "spec_version": "1.3",
125	  "generation_date": "{ISO-8601}",
126	  "description": "{SKILL.md frontmatter description}",
127	  "language": "{primary-source-language}",
128	  "ast_node_count": "{number-or-omitted-if-no-ast}",
129	  "exports": [],
130	  "tool_versions": {
131	    "ast_grep": "{version-or-null}",
132	    "qmd": "{version-or-null}",
133	    "skf": "{skf_version}"
134	  },
135	  "confidence_distribution": {
136	    "t1": 0,
137	    "t1_low": 0,
138	    "t2": 0,
139	    "t3": 0
140	  },
141	  "stats": {
142	    "exports_documented": 0,
143	    "exports_public_api": 0,
144	    "exports_internal": 0,
145	    "exports_total": 0,
146	    "public_api_coverage": 0.0,
147	    "total_coverage": 0.0,
148	    "scripts_count": 0,
149	    "assets_count": 0
150	  },
151	  // scripts[] and assets[] — include ONLY when inventories are non-empty; omit entirely otherwise
152	  // "scripts": [{ "file": "scripts/{name}", "purpose": "{description}", "source_file": "{source-path}", "confidence": "T1-low" }],
153	  // "assets": [{ "file": "assets/{name}", "purpose": "{description}", "source_file": "{source-path}", "confidence": "T1-low" }],
154	  "generated_by": "{quick-skill|create-skill}",
155	  "dependencies": [],
156	  "compatibility": "{semver-range}"
157	}
158	```
159	
160	---
161	
162	## references/ Directory Structure
163	
164	One file per major function group or type:
165	
166	```
167	references/
168	├── {function-group-a}.md    — Detailed reference with full examples
169	├── {function-group-b}.md    — Detailed reference with full examples
170	└── {type-name}.md           — Type definition details
171	```
172	
173	Each reference file includes:
174	- Full function signatures
175	- Detailed parameter descriptions
176	- Return value details
177	- Complete usage examples
178	- Related functions cross-references
179	- Temporal annotations (Deep tier: T2-past, T2-future)
180	- **Table of contents** (required for files exceeding 100 lines) — a `## Contents` section at the top listing all sub-sections for agent discoverability
181	
182	---
183	
184	## provenance-map.json Structure
185	
186	```json
187	{
188	  "skill_name": "{name}",
189	  "source_repo": "{url}",
190	  "source_commit": "{hash}",
191	  "generated_at": "{ISO-8601}",
192	  "entries": [
193	    {
194	      "export_name": "getToken",
195	      "export_type": "function",
196	      "params": ["userId: string", "options?: TokenOptions"],
197	      "return_type": "Token",
198	      "source_file": "src/auth/index.ts",
199	      "source_line": 42,
200	      "confidence": "T1",
201	      "extraction_method": "ast-grep",
202	      "ast_node_type": "export_function_declaration",
203	      "signature_source": "T1"
204	    }
205	  ],
206	  "file_entries": [
207	    {
208	      "file_name": "scripts/{name}",
209	      "file_type": "script",
210	      "source_file": "{source-path}",
211	      "confidence": "T1-low",
212	      "extraction_method": "file-copy",
213	      "content_hash": "sha256:{hash}"
214	    }
215	  ]
216	}
217	```
218	
219	`scripts` and `assets` arrays in metadata.json are optional — omit entirely (not empty arrays) when no scripts/assets exist. `file_entries` in provenance-map.json is optional — omit when no scripts/assets exist.
220	
221	---
222	
223	## evidence-report.md Structure
224	
225	```markdown
226	# Evidence Report: {skill-name}
227	
228	**Generated:** {date}
229	**Forge Tier:** {tier}
230	**Source:** {repo} @ {commit}
231	
232	## Tool Versions
233	- ast-grep: {version}
234	- QMD: {version}
235	- SKF: {skf_version}
236	
237	## Extraction Summary
238	- Files scanned: {count}
239	- Exports found: {count}
240	- Confidence: T1={n}, T1-low={n}, T2={n}, T3={n}
241	
242	## Validation Results
243	- Schema: {pass/fail}
244	- Frontmatter: {pass/fail}
245	
246	## Warnings
247	- {any warnings from extraction or validation}
248	```

1	# SKILL.md Compilation Assembly Rules
2	
3	## Frontmatter (REQUIRED — agentskills.io compliance)
4	
5	```yaml
6	---
7	name: {brief.name}
8	description: >
9	  {Trigger-optimized description from brief and extraction data.
10	  Include what it does, when to use it, and what NOT to use it for.
11	  1-1024 chars, optimized for agent discovery.}
12	---
13	```
14	
15	**Frontmatter rules:**
16	
17	- `name`: lowercase alphanumeric + hyphens only, must match the skill output directory name. Prefer gerund form (`processing-pdfs`, `analyzing-spreadsheets`) for clarity.
18	- `description`: non-empty, max 1024 chars, optimized for agent discovery. **MUST use third-person voice** ("Processes Excel files..." not "I can help you..." or "You can use this to..."). Inconsistent point-of-view causes discovery problems since the description is injected into the system prompt.
19	- Only `name` and `description` in frontmatter — `version` and `author` go in metadata.json
20	- No other frontmatter fields for standard skills (only `name`, `description`, `license`, `compatibility`, `metadata`, `allowed-tools` are permitted by spec)
21	
22	## Two-Tier Assembly
23	
24	**CRITICAL: Two-tier assembly.** SKILL.md must retain actionable inline content that survives `split-body` extraction. Assemble Tier 1 sections first (always inline), then Tier 2 sections (reference-eligible, may be extracted by split-body).
25	
26	### Tier 1 — Always Inline (must survive split-body)
27	
28	These sections form the essential standalone body. Target: **under 300 lines total** for Tier 1. An agent loading only SKILL.md (without references) must get enough to act.
29	
30	**Section 1 — Overview (~10 lines):**
31	- 1-line summary of what the library does
32	- Source repo, version, branch
33	- Forge tier used for compilation
34	- Export count and confidence summary
35	
36	**Section 2 — Quick Start (~30 lines):**
37	- Select the 3-5 most commonly used functions (by import frequency or documentation prominence)
38	- One runnable code example showing a typical end-to-end flow (e.g., `add → process → search`)
39	- Minimal usage examples — ONLY from source tests or official docs
40	- If no examples exist in source, show signature-only quick start
41	- Provenance citation for each function
42	
43	**Section 3 — Common Workflows (~30 lines):**
44	- 4-5 patterns showing typical function call sequences
45	- Each pattern: 1-line bold description + function call chain with key params
46	- Focus on the most common developer tasks, not exhaustive coverage
47	- Format example:
48	  ```
49	  **Add and process data:**
50	  `await cognee.add(data) → await cognee.cognify() → await cognee.search(query)`
51	  ```
52	
53	**Section 4 — Key API Summary (~20 lines):**
54	- Table of top 10-15 functions: name, purpose, key parameters
55	- One row per function — no full signatures, just enough for discovery
56	- Provenance citation per function
57	
58	**Section 4b — Migration & Deprecation Warnings (~10 lines, Deep tier only):**
59	- Only populated when step-04 enrichment produced **T2-future** annotations (deprecation warnings, breaking changes, planned renames)
60	- List each warning as a single-line bullet: function name, what changed or will change, source citation
61	- Max 10 lines — just the actionable warnings, not full context
62	- Link to Tier 2 Full API Reference for details: "See Full API Reference for migration details."
63	- **Skip entirely** for Quick/Forge tiers or when no T2-future annotations exist — do not emit an empty section
64	- This section survives split-body, ensuring agents always see critical migration context
65	
66	**Section 5 — Key Types (~20 lines):**
67	- Most important enum/type definitions inline (e.g., SearchType values, config options)
68	- Only types that appear in Quick Start or Common Workflows
69	- Full type details go in Tier 2
70	
71	**Section 6 — Architecture at a Glance (~10 lines):**
72	- Bullet list of major subsystem categories (e.g., "Graph DBs: Neo4j, Kuzu, Neptune")
73	- Adapter/driver overview — what's available, not how it works
74	- Skip for Quick tier or small libraries with < 5 modules
75	
76	**Section 7 — CLI (~10 lines, if applicable):**
77	- Basic CLI commands if the library has a CLI interface
78	- Skip if no CLI exists
79	
80	**Section 7b — Scripts & Assets (~10 lines, if applicable):**
81	- Manifest table of included scripts with filename, one-line purpose, and provenance citation
82	- Manifest table of included assets with filename, one-line purpose, and provenance citation
83	- Each entry: `scripts/{filename}` or `assets/{filename}`, purpose, `[SRC:{source_path}:L1]`
84	- Include a note: "Load scripts from `scripts/` and assets from `assets/` when directed by the instructions above."
85	- **Skip entirely** when no scripts or assets detected in extraction inventory — do not emit an empty section
86	- Like Sections 4b and 7, parsers must treat this section as optional
87	
88	**Section 8 — Manual Sections:**
89	- Seed empty `<!-- [MANUAL] -->` markers:
90	```markdown
91	<!-- [MANUAL:additional-notes] -->
92	<!-- Add custom notes here. This section is preserved during skill updates. -->
93	<!-- [/MANUAL:additional-notes] -->
94	```
95	- Place after Quick Start and after Key API Summary sections
96	
97	### Tier 2 — Reference-Eligible (can be extracted by split-body)
98	
99	Assemble Sections 9-11 (Full API Reference, Full Type Definitions, Full Integration Patterns) as defined in the skill-sections data file. These contain full detail and are split into `references/` when the body exceeds 500 lines. Include T2 annotations from enrichment in the Full API Reference (Deep tier only).
100	
101	**CRITICAL — Tier 2 differentiation from Tier 1:** Tier 2 Full API Reference must contain content that is NOT present in Tier 1's Key API Summary. Specifically:
102	
103	- **Full parameter tables** with types, defaults, and required/optional flags (Tier 1 only lists key params)
104	- **Return value details** including structure, types, and error conditions
105	- **T2 temporal annotations** — migration notes, deprecation details, breaking change context (Deep tier)
106	- **Usage examples** from source tests or documentation (Tier 1 has signature-only references)
107	- **Edge cases and constraints** — parameter validation rules, size limits, behavioral notes
108	
109	Do NOT repeat Tier 1's name/purpose/key-params table format in Tier 2. Tier 2 is a deep reference, not a reformatted summary. This distinction prevents conciseness scorers from flagging the two-tier design as redundancy.
110	
111	### Assembly Rules
112	
113	1. Assemble all Tier 1 sections first — these form the essential standalone body
114	2. Assemble all Tier 2 sections after — these are progressive disclosure detail
115	3. Tier 1 content MUST be under 300 lines (excluding frontmatter)
116	4. If Tier 1 alone exceeds 300 lines, reduce Key API Summary and Architecture at a Glance
117	5. Tier 1 sections are kept short enough that `split-body` targets the larger Tier 2 sections (`## Full ...` headings) instead
118	6. After split-body, SKILL.md must still contain all Tier 1 sections with actionable content
119	7. **Signature fidelity:** When populating function entries from the extraction inventory, always use the `params` and `return_type` fields from the provenance-map entry (T1/AST-backed). Do not substitute parameter names, types, or return types from documentation, README examples, or enrichment annotations. T2/T3 sources may enrich descriptions and add usage notes, but structural signature data from AST extraction is authoritative.
120	
121	### Reference File Rules
122	
123	- **Table of contents required** for any reference file exceeding 100 lines — include a `## Contents` section at the top listing all sub-sections. This ensures agents can see the full scope even when previewing with partial reads.
124	- One file per major function group or type — group by module, file, or functional area
125	- Name files descriptively: `form_validation_rules.md`, not `doc2.md`
126	
127	### Content Quality Rules
128	
129	These rules apply to all content assembled in SKILL.md and reference files.
130	
131	**Degrees of freedom:** Match instruction specificity to the task's fragility and variability:
132	- **High freedom** (text guidance): When multiple approaches are valid and context determines the best one. Example: code review patterns, architecture suggestions.
133	- **Medium freedom** (pseudocode/parameterized scripts): When a preferred pattern exists but variation is acceptable. Example: configuration templates, report generation.
134	- **Low freedom** (exact scripts, no parameters): When operations are fragile and consistency is critical. Example: database migrations, deployment sequences. Use "Run exactly this" language.
135	
136	**Consistent terminology:** Choose one term per concept and use it throughout the skill. Do not mix synonyms (e.g., "API endpoint" vs "URL" vs "route", or "field" vs "box" vs "element"). Consistency helps agents understand and follow instructions deterministically.
137	
138	**Avoid time-sensitive information:** Do not include date-conditional instructions ("If before August 2025, use the old API"). Instead, document the current method and place deprecated patterns in a collapsible "Old patterns" section with the deprecation date.
139	
140	**Template and examples patterns:**
141	- **For strict requirements** (API responses, data formats): Provide an exact template with "ALWAYS use this exact structure" language.
142	- **For flexible guidance** (reports, analysis): Provide a sensible default template with "Adjust sections as needed" language.
143	- **Input/output examples:** When output quality depends on seeing examples and concrete pairs exist in source tests or official docs, include 2-3 input/output pairs sourced from those tests or docs. Examples help agents understand desired style and detail more clearly than descriptions alone. If no examples exist in source, note the gap rather than fabricating pairs — zero hallucination applies.
144	
145	**Workflow checklist pattern:** When a skill includes multi-step workflows, provide a copy-paste checklist that agents can track progress against:
146	```markdown
147	Copy this checklist and track your progress:
148	- [ ] Step 1: {action}
149	- [ ] Step 2: {action}
150	```
151	
152	**Verifiable intermediate outputs:** For skills involving batch operations, destructive changes, or complex validation, recommend the plan-validate-execute pattern: create a structured plan file (e.g., `changes.json`), validate it with a script, then execute. This catches errors before changes are applied.
153	
```

Search the filtered file list for files matching these patterns (in priority order):\n   - Files named `registry.ts`, `registry.tsx`, `registry.js` in any directory\n   - Files named `components.ts`, `components.tsx` in `registry/`, `catalog/`, or `components/` directories\n   - Files named `index.ts`, `index.tsx` in `registry/` or `catalog/` directories\n\n2. For each candidate, read the file and look for:\n   - Arrays of objects with fields like `id`, `name`, `component`, `category`\n   - Type annotations containing `Component[]` or similar array types\n   - 10+ entries to qualify as a registry (reject small arrays)\n\n3. **Confidence scoring** for each candidate:\n   - +3 points: Contains `id` field per entry\n   - +2 points: Contains `name` or `component` field per entry\n   - +2 points: Contains `category` or `tags` field per entry\n   - +1 point: Located in a `registry/` or `catalog/` directory\n   - +1 point: Has 20+ entries\n   - Minimum threshold: 5 points to qualify\n\n4. **User confirmation required:**\n\n\"**Registry candidate detected** at `{path}` (confidence: {score}/9, {count} entries).\n\nSample entries:\n{show 3-5 sample entries with id, name, category}\n\nIs this the component registry? Options:\n- **[P]** Provide the registry file path\n- **[S]** Skip registry — proceed with standard props-first extraction only\"\n\nWait for user response.\n\n### Phase 3: Parse Registry\n\nIf a registry was confirmed, parse it into `component_catalog[]`:\n\n**Per-component entry:**\n\n- `id` — registry key (used for CLI `add` command)\n- `name` — display name (PascalCase)\n- `description` — from registry or empty\n- `category` — from registry field or directory structure\n- `available_in[]` — which design system variants include this component\n- `code_paths[]` — source file path for each variant\n- `tags[]` — from registry or empty\n- Provenance citation: `[SRC:{registry_file}:L{line}]`\n\nDisplay: \"**Parsed component catalog: {N} components across {M} categories.**\"\n\n**If no registry:** Set `component_catalog: []` and proceed to Phase 4.\n\n### Phase 4: Props-First Extraction\n\nExtract props interfaces as the primary API contracts, then link to components.\n\n**Step 1 — Extract Props interfaces:**\n\nLoad `{extractionPatternsData}` and use the component-library-specific patterns.\n\nUsing AST tools (Forge/Deep) or source reading (Quick):\n\n```yaml\n# React/TypeScript props interfaces\nid: react-props-interfaces\nlanguage: typescript\nrule:\n  pattern: 'export interface $NAME { $$$ }'\nconstraints:\n  NAME:\n    regex: '.*Props$'\n```\n\nFor each `*Props` interface found:\n- Extract all fields with types, optionality, and default values\n- Extract JSDoc descriptions per field (if present)\n- Record: interface name, fields[], source file, line number\n- Provenance: `[AST:{file}:L{line}]` or `[SRC:{file}:L{line}]`\n\n**Step 2 — Extract component exports:**\n\n```yaml\n# React component exports (PascalCase)\nid: react-component-exports\nlanguage: tsx  # Use 'tsx' for .tsx files, 'typescript' for .ts files\nrule:\n  pattern: 'export function $NAME($$$PARAMS)'\nconstraints:\n  NAME:\n    regex: '^[A-Z]'\n```\n\nAlso run `export const $NAME` patterns for arrow function components.\n\nFor each component export: record name, source file, line number. Do NOT document the function signature in detail (it's always `(props: XProps) => JSX.Element`).\n\n**Step 3 — Link Props to Components:**\n\nUse a 3-level fallback chain:\n\n1. **Naming convention (primary):** Match `FooProps` → `Foo` component by stripping the `Props` suffix\n2. **File co-location (fallback):** If naming doesn't match, check if a Props interface and a component are defined in the same file\n3. **Generic parameter (deep fallback):** Look for `ComponentProps<typeof Foo>` or similar generic patterns that reference the component\n\nFor each linked pair, record the association. For unlinked Props interfaces, include them as standalone type exports.\n\n**Step 4 — Extract shared types:**\n\nExtract non-Props type exports using standard AST patterns (same as Forge tier):\n- `export type $NAME = $VALUE`\n- `export enum $NAME { $$$ }`\n- `export interface $NAME { $$$ }` (excluding `*Props` already captured)\n\n### Phase 5: Variant Consolidation\n\n**Skip this phase if `scope.ui_variants` is not specified and no variant directories detected.**\n\nWhen multiple design system variants exist:\n\n1. **Group components by registry `id`** (NOT by filename — registry is source of truth):\n   - For each `id` in `component_catalog[]`, collect all variant paths from `available_in[]` and `code_paths[]`\n\n2. **Select canonical props definition:**\n   - Use the primary variant (first in `scope.ui_variants` list) as canonical\n   - If primary variant's props are unavailable, use the first available variant\n\n3. **Detect props differences between variants:**\n   - For components available in 2+ variants, compare Props interfaces\n   - Record any variant-specific props as notes (e.g., \"Base UI variant adds `slots` prop\")\n\n4. **Deduplicate export counts:**\n   - Count unique components (by registry `id`), not total files across variants\n   - Record: `components_unique: {N}`, `components_total_with_variants: {M}`\n\nDisplay: \"**Variant consolidation: {unique} unique components across {variant_count} variants** ({total} total including variants). Primary variant: {primary_name}.\"\n\n### Phase 6: Build Component Extraction Results\n\nCompile all extracted data into the format expected by step-03 section 5:\n\n**Per-export entry (for Props interfaces — primary API):**\n\n- Interface name (e.g., `NativeLiquidButtonProps`)\n- Full interface with all fields and types\n- Parameters: each field as name, type, required/optional, default\n- Linked component name (e.g., `NativeLiquidButton`)\n- Source file and line number\n- Provenance citation\n- Confidence tier (T1 or T1-low)\n\n**Per-export entry (for component functions):**\n\n- Component name\n- Linked Props interface (if found)\n- Source file and line number\n- Provenance citation\n- Confidence tier\n\n**Per-export entry (for shared types):**\n\n- Same as standard extraction format\n\n**Component library aggregate counts:**\n\n- `components_registered`: count from registry (or 0)\n- `components_documented`: count of components with linked Props\n- `props_interfaces_extracted`: count of `*Props` interfaces\n- `components_unique`: deduplicated count (after variant consolidation)\n- `demo_files_excluded`: count from Phase 1\n- `design_variants`: map of variant name → component count (if variants exist)\n\n**Store `component_catalog[]` in context** — this is consumed by step-05 for the Component Catalog section.\n\nDisplay: \"**Component extraction complete.** Returning to main extraction flow.\"\n\n## RETURN PROTOCOL\n\nAfter Phase 6 completes, return control to step-03 section 5 (Build Extraction Inventory). The extraction results from this step are merged into the standard extraction inventory format. Step-03 continues with its normal Gate 2 summary and menu.\n\nDo NOT load `{returnToStep}` — the calling step (step-03) will continue from where it delegated.\n\n---\n\n## SYSTEM SUCCESS/FAILURE METRICS\n\n### SUCCESS:\n\n- Demo/example files detected and excluded with user confirmation\n- Registry detected (or user provided path) and parsed into component_catalog\n- Props interfaces extracted as primary API contracts\n- Components linked to Props via 3-level fallback chain\n- Variant consolidation produced deduplicated counts (if applicable)\n- All extracted items have provenance citations\n- Results compatible with step-03 section 5 inventory format\n- component_catalog stored in context for step-05\n\n### FAILURE:\n\n- Excluding demo files without user confirmation\n- Accepting a registry candidate without user confirmation\n- Not extracting Props interfaces as primary API surface\n- Documenting component function signatures instead of Props\n- Including hallucinated component data not in source\n- Variant deduplication based on filenames instead of registry IDs\n- Not returning control to step-03 after completion\n\n**Master Rule:** Zero hallucination — every extraction must trace to source code. Full Props details go in Tier 2.\n\n**Tier 2 (Full API Reference) — Props Reference override:**\n\nOrganize by component (not by function). Per component:\n\n```markdown\n### {ComponentName}\n\n**Install:** `npx {cli} add {component-id}`\n**Available in:** {variant list}\n**Props:** `{ComponentName}Props`\n\n| Prop | Type | Default | Description |\n|------|------|---------|-------------|\n| {prop} | {type} | {default} | {JSDoc description or —} |\n```\n\n**context-snippet.md — Component Library format:**\n\n```markdown\n[{name} v{version}]|root: skills/{name}/\n|IMPORTANT: {name} v{version} — read SKILL.md before writing {name} code. Do NOT rely on training data.\n|install: npx {cli} add <component-id>\n|catalog:{SKILL.md#component-catalog} — {N} components: {category(count), ...}\n|variants: {variant list} — {provider wrapping note if applicable}\n|key-props:{SKILL.md#key-types} — {top props interfaces with key fields}\n|gotchas: {detected gotchas}\n```\n\n**metadata.json — Component Library stats:**\n\nWhen `scope.type: \"component-library\"`, add these fields to `stats`:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"stats\": {\n    \"components_registered\": 0,\n    \"components_documented\": 0,\n    \"props_interfaces_extracted\": 0,\n    \"components_unique\": 0,\n    \"demo_files_excluded\": 0,\n    \"design_variants\": {}\n  }\n}\n```\n\nThese are in addition to the standard stats fields (exports_documented, etc.).\n\n### Assembly Rules"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md",
  "old_string": "### Known ast-grep Limitations",
  "new_string": "### Component Library YAML Rule Recipes\n\nThese patterns are used by `step-03d-component-extraction.md` when `scope.type: \"component-library\"`. They prioritize Props interfaces and PascalCase component exports.\n\n**React/TypeScript — Props interfaces (primary API contracts):**\n\n```yaml\nid: react-props-interfaces\nlanguage: typescript  # Use 'tsx' for .tsx files\nrule:\n  pattern: 'export interface $NAME { $$$ }'\nconstraints:\n  NAME:\n    regex: '.*Props$'\n```\n\n**React/TypeScript — Component function exports (PascalCase):**\n\n> **Language note:** Use `language: tsx` for `.tsx` files. The `export function` pattern may fail with tsx on ast-grep 0.41.x (see Known Limitations #5). Use `export const` patterns as primary and fall back to source reading for `export function` in tsx files.\n\n```yaml\nid: react-component-functions\nlanguage: tsx\nrule:\n  pattern: 'export function $NAME($$$PARAMS)'\nconstraints:\n  NAME:\n    regex: '^[A-Z]'\n```\n\n**React/TypeScript — Component arrow function exports:**\n\n```yaml\nid: react-component-arrow-functions\nlanguage: typescript\nrule:\n  pattern: 'export const $NAME = ($$$PARAMS) => $BODY'\nconstraints:\n  NAME:\n    regex: '^[A-Z]'\n```\n\n**Vue — defineProps extraction:**\n\n```yaml\nid: vue-define-props\nlanguage: typescript\nrule:\n  pattern: 'defineProps<$TYPE>()'\n```\n\n**Props-to-Component linking strategy:**\n\nAfter extracting Props interfaces and component exports, link them using this 3-level fallback chain:\n\n1. **Naming convention (primary):** Strip `Props` suffix from interface name → match to component export (e.g., `NativeLiquidButtonProps` → `NativeLiquidButton`)\n2. **File co-location (fallback):** If naming doesn't match, check if a Props interface and a PascalCase export function are defined in the same file — link them\n3. **Generic parameter (deep fallback):** Search for `ComponentProps<typeof $NAME>` or `React.ComponentProps<typeof $NAME>` patterns that reference the component by name\n\nUnlinked Props interfaces are included as standalone type exports. Unlinked component exports are included with a note that no Props interface was found (signature-only, T1-low confidence for API contract).\n\n### Known ast-grep Limitations"
}
```
